### 0.7.21.3-7p - Push preparation: the size, fit and phone-height stages reviewed, and ADR-0024 decision 6 rewritten - 14 October 2026

- The commits 0.7.21.3 to 0.7.0.48 had been pushed one by one without a push preparation, so this preparation reviewed the whole span since 0.7.19.19-29p together with 0.7.21.6 and 0.7.21.7. An independent review of the code diff found the following, all fixed before the push.
- «Size px» and «Length» on a shape divided by the content width, which is `"full"` on a full-width site, so a value typed there wrote `NaN` frames into the draft. The editor now takes the canvas's design width the way the engine does (`designWidth()`: the site's width, or the section's own px width), shows the px fields only where there is one, and keeps a line's length in percent where there is not.
- The phone loading reserve (`--urd-loading-h`) stood inside the content that the block's «Size» zooms, so the home calendar at 70 % reserved 70 % of its height and still jumped. The reserve is now written in the content's own px (divided by the size).
- A phone height measured for an edit could land after an undo, a redo or a publish (up to about 5 s later), writing the undone design's height into the restored draft and leaving it dirty. `dropFits` now also forgets the phone requests (`phoneSeq`, `phoneFit.cancel()`).
- The size slider scaled the frame step by step, so a slide up and back changed the frame for good once the width met the canvas edge, and the blocks below were pushed down but never back. One slider gesture is now computed from where it began (`sizeGesture`).
- ADR-0024 decision 6 still described the shrink as zooming only as far as the design height needs, which is `fit: "both"` since 0.7.21.5. Decision 6 is rewritten in place for the three choices, with the earlier text in `docs/adr/amended/0024-content-push-on-the-desktop-canvas-until-2026-10-14.md`.
- Smaller findings fixed: a section width that is not in px gave the proportional shrink a design width it did not have (engine and editor now treat it as none, and «Shrink the content» is offered only where there is a design width); a template saved before page v6 kept the calendar's `props.scale` (the 5 → 6 step is now `liftCalendarScale`, used by template insertion too); SCHEMA.md stopped at the 4 → 5 migration and showed `"schemaVersion": 5`; seven code comments cited ADR addenda that 0.7.0.44 had folded into the decisions; the size readout was not listed as editing chrome, so it counted as content during a drag; the hidden phone preview was not `inert`, and kept the plugin list it started with; `lbl.height` stood twice in four dictionaries since 0.7.21.3; the dev server tested Linux's errno 98 for a busy port instead of `errno.EADDRINUSE`; and an orphaned doc comment sat above `seasonRange` in ics.js.
- The review also noted that the example data has no `mobileHeight`. The field is measured, so it is left without a value: an unmeasured number would make the jump the field prevents, and the editor fills it at the next edit of the home calendar.

### 0.7.21.7 - The phone height of a block that fetches its content, measured by the editor so the first load on a phone does not jump - 14 October 2026

- A calendar, collection or product block on a phone had no known height until its content was drawn, so the first load of a session jumped (measured 298 px while loading against 353 px drawn). The editor now asks a hidden preview of the page at the phone width for the block's height after every edit that can change it (`editor/src/lib/phone-fit.js`, through the existing `urd-fit-block`) and stores it as the additive block field `mobileHeight` (schema in three places; `FETCHES_CONTENT` and `mobileLoadingHeight` in push-model.js, with tests). On the phone the loading state stands at it (`--urd-loading-h`), and the calendar keeps preferring the height the session has seen on the device.
- The collection and product blocks are now marked busy while they fetch their entries, as the calendar was: the reserve applies only while loading, the desktop holds the frame's height meanwhile, and the editor's fit waits for the content.

### 0.7.21.6 - Drags that show their result: Shift keeps the ratio, a size readout, the content stop on the phone, and the toolbar of a rotated block clear of its content - 14 October 2026

- A picture, video, shape or icon could only be resized freely, so its proportions were lost by hand; Shift now keeps the ratio on those four (`FIT_BY_WIDTH`), the width driving and the grid snapping as usual, and their handle says so. Shift stays «free» on the other block types.
- Nothing showed the size while dragging: a readout under the block (`sizeReadout` in preview-edit.js, width in percent and height in px) follows the drag on the desktop and the phone, and shows the drawn height of a block that follows its content.
- In the mobile row grid a block that follows its content could be dragged to any height; it now keeps its height there too, the handle changing the width and a pull up or down showing the same stop bar as on the desktop (ADR-0025).
- Test finding the same day, fixed before the commit: the toolbar of a rotated block was anchored at the block's own corner and turned upright there, so it lay over the content whenever that corner was not the block's highest point. It is now anchored at the block's bounding box on the screen, above or flipped under (`toolbarAnchor` in place.js, with tests), and follows a rotation drag.

### 0.7.0.48 - Design from other tools: a survey, and the milestones 0.7.25 «Own looks from design tools» and v0.8 «Animation files» - 14 October 2026

- The comparison maps had Lottie, Rive, Spline, custom HTML and iframes as Not taken, with no answer for a site owner who designs an element elsewhere. A survey of 14 October 2026 (SURVEYS.md «Design from other tools»: what each tool exports for self-hosting, the players' licences, sizes and CSP needs, sandboxed frames on Cloudflare Pages, design tokens, 9-slice faces, SVG sanitising and pointer effects) gave three tracks, written as two milestones: 0.7.25 «Own looks from design tools» (the pointer as custom properties, an allowlist sanitiser for inline SVG, faces by `border-image` with state files, scoped CSS styles pasted whole, a tokens importer with a mapping step) and v0.8 «Animation files» (Lottie, Rive and glTF as files in `media/` with vendored MIT players as shipped plugins, and a sandboxed portal with a CSP of its own for exported code). Spline's runtime is never vendored (no licence); Framer, Figma Sites and Penpot prototypes have no self-hostable export. Both roadmaps follow; the maps' statuses change, with new rows E38-E40 and T114-T116.
- A 0.7.11 item: animated images (GIF, WebP, APNG and animated SVG) keep moving under reduced motion and cannot be paused.

### 0.7.21.5 - «Size» for every block with the frame following it, three fit choices below the design width, a px size for shapes, and the comparison's 0.7 findings sorted into the backlog - 14 October 2026

- The calendar alone had a «Size» (`props.scale`, in the Style tab), and no other block could be drawn larger or smaller than its design. Every block but the frame-sized types (image, video, shape, icon, map) now takes a block-level `size` (0.4 to 2) in the Placement area, drawn as a zoom of the content on the desktop and the phone alike and multiplied with the shrink below the design width (ADR-0025 decision 6); the base text stays inside «Smallest text» and «Largest text» (`sizeMin`, `sizeMax`, defaults 12 and 96 px), shown only while a size is set. The calendar's `scale` is read into `size` by the page migration 5 → 6 (`migrate.js`, the example pages at schemaVersion 6), and the calendar's own zoom is gone. Schema in three places, `blockSize` and `sizeBounds` in push-model.js with tests.
- Test findings on the same day, fixed before the commit: the two text bounds stood as two full rows and made the menu taller, so they stand side by side in the frame grid; a size changed only the zoom inside a fixed frame, so the text wrapped more as it grew, and the editor now scales the frame with the size (the width by the ratio, capped at the canvas edge, the height where the block owns it with the blocks below moved as for a fit; a block that follows its content is fitted after); the text bounds were computed against the size times the shrink, so each shrink step raised the text's floor, the lines changed and the fit's measure swung between wrapping and unwrapping, and the bounds now hold against the size alone.
- «Shrink the content» both shrank and wrapped: the fit zoomed the content only as much as the design height needed, and a larger zoom with one more line can fit that height. `fit` now has three choices: wrap (as before), shrink (the zoom is the canvas width over the design width, `canvasRatio` in render.js, so the lines never change down to the floor and nothing is measured) and the new «Shrink and wrap the content» (`fit: "both"`, the old bisection). A stored `shrink` takes the proportional shrink; the schema, SCHEMA.md and the tooltip describe the three. Found 14 October 2026 that the shrink does not reach the phone's row grid; it is a 0.7.3 item.
- Shapes had no size apart from the frame's percent width: «Size px» in the shape's Content area sets the width in px at the design width with the height following the shape's ratio, and a line's «Length» is in px instead of percent. The «Size» tooltip is rewritten in plain words (the whole element zoomed, 100 % as drawn, never changed by a drag).
- The comparison maps were read for what fits v0.7 and the backlog took it: a site-wide pause for motion, an audio transcript and Alt+Arrow moves announced to screen readers in 0.7.11; sorting, active-filter chips, a count, a category list and A-Z anchors in 0.7.16.3; page setup and page breaks in 0.7.22; `scroll-state`, `moveBefore()` and `text-fit` on the 0.7.0 watch list; HSTS and the eager first picture moved from v0.8 to 0.7.0, the alt-text list from the v0.8 media library to the 0.7.11 audit panel, and the announcement bar's date window to 0.7.13.18; to be weighed in their milestones: desktop and phone side by side (0.7.3), a vision simulation (0.7.11), inheritance marks in the element menu (0.7.20) and a voluntary accessibility page (0.7.24.2). The statuses in the maps and the v0.8 lines of both roadmaps follow.

AGENTS.md (Workflow) now asks for the item's IDs, category, element row, design pattern and CODE.md's platform gates to be read before work starts, a finding that changes the item to be written into it first, and a delivered recommendation to get the status Delivered with its commit number at «commit».

They are replaced by `docs/comparison/` in English: README (the index, source marks, ID scheme and a map from every old number), FEATURES (C1-C114), ELEMENTS (E1-E37), DESIGN (D1-D62, new), CODE (T1-T113) and SURVEYS (the dated surveys the ADRs cite, kept as decided).

The archiving ADR of 0.7.16.1 no longer claims ADR-0025, which is the sizing model.

### 0.7.0.45 - Bug round: the calendar's parser, Year wheel, phone width and Band pause, media in the CSP, and the dev server in the template - 11 October 2026

- An unpublished film or sound file (a data URL in the draft) was blocked in a deployed site's preview, since `_headers` had no `media-src`. The CSP allows `media-src 'self' data:` (no `blob:`, which no media path uses); the pinned released blob in `tests/release-headers.test.mjs` follows, and existing sites get the line as the updater's «change by hand» note.
- A «Coming up» design's countdown left a minute timer running after every redraw. `everyMinute` (blocks/calendar-next.js) stops the timers whose node a redraw has taken off the page before it starts a new one.
- The parser counted a weekly rule's weeks from Sunday and ignored `WKST`, kept a yearly rule with `BYDAY` or `BYMONTHDAY` and no `BYMONTH` to the start's month, and ignored `BYSETPOS` in a weekly rule, so such events landed on other days than in Google, Outlook and Apple. Weeks start on `WKST` (Monday by default), the yearly forms span the year as RFC 5545 §3.3.10 reads them, and `BYSETPOS` picks among a week's days; rules with `BYWEEKNO` or `BYYEARDAY`, which stay unsupported, keep the start's month. Tested with the RFC's own examples.
- The Year wheel's «month at the top» turned the ring but kept the calendar year, so with August at the top the months before it showed this year's past events. The wheel reads the season from its top month that holds today, labelled «2026/27» (`seasonRange` and `seasonTop` in ics.js, also the window the block loads), and a month is past by its place in the season.
- Test finding: a press on the wheel's months did nothing in the editor's preview, since the editing layer started a drag of the block; the month names took no presses at all, the names at the sides were clipped by the drawing's edge, and the wheel jumped when the month's list beside it changed height. The months and their names keep their press as a calendar's events do (preview-edit.js), the names pick the month, the drawing shows past its edge, and the wheel stands at the top.
- Calendar designs ran out of a phone's width: the column of List on navy's sign-up, the title sizes of Regular event, Coming up on navy and Billboard, a word wider than the block in 19 designs, and a search field of about 320 px. The calendar is one column as wide as its block, its text fields break a word that does not fit (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), the search field follows the block, the poster tracks follow it too, and the five designs get phone rules. Measured at 360 and 320 px with long titles, places and a 48-letter word, every design stays inside its block; the first fix broke the titles of List on navy and Coming up on navy inside ordinary words, which their tighter phone layouts settled.
- The Band design paused only under the pointer and while focus was inside it, which WCAG 2.2.2 does not count as a pause, and a touch could not pause it. The design's new option «Pause» (shown while «Roll» is on) gives a pause button at the band's edge, or a pause on touch with the button there for the keyboard; a pause stays across redraws. Under reduced motion the band stands still and scrolls sideways instead of clipping its events.
- The printed calendar list ran over the next block on paper. It needs a print flow for the whole page and becomes milestone 0.7.22 in BACKLOG and both roadmaps.
- Test finding: after the wheel fix the months still did not respond until the browser's cache was emptied, since the site was served by `python3 -m http.server`, which the template's README recommended and which lets the browser keep old engine files. The dev server ships in the template as `template/dev-server.py` (Urd-owned, serving its own folder with caching off), the root `dev-server.py` starts it, and the template's READMEs, the plugin guide, DEVELOPMENT, UTVIKLING and AGENTS.md point to it; a BACKLOG line claiming the dev server routes the page registry was corrected.

### 0.7.0.44 - The ADRs as standing decisions, with the mechanics in ARCHITECTURE, SCHEMA and DEVELOPMENT - 11 October 2026

- The 25 ADRs had grown build mechanics, sequences of events and addenda that changed decisions without rewriting them (the rule in AGENTS.md, set 11 October 2026). All are reviewed and 22 edited: nine rewritten where a decision had changed (0004, 0005, 0008, 0011, 0012, 0013, 0016, 0018, 0022; the earlier text of each, and of 0021 and 0023 whose text was restructured, is kept under `docs/adr/amended/`), the rest trimmed; 0001, 0002 and 0010 were clean. The ADRs go from about 25 000 to 17 500 words.
- The mechanics the ADRs carried are now described where they belong: ARCHITECTURE.md (the migration machinery, the sanitiser, the help chip, the dictionary loading, the MCP server's root, the mobile row grid, the navigation bar's sizing, the push pass in full, and the editor's themed components, floating menus, panel-language class contract, own ids, Screen device, canvas measuring and mobile messages), SCHEMA.md (the `accent-text` automatics and a «The page migrations» section with all five steps and the English name map) and DEVELOPMENT-en-GB.md with UTVIKLING.md (the invariants pinned by tests, the Norwegian audit, the payment layer's secrets, the release steps in full).
- Three references to withdrawn addenda (ARCHITECTURE.md, AGENTS.md, BACKLOG.md) point at the decisions instead.

### 0.7.21.3 - «Follow the content» or «Own height» per block, and the section's own height is the owner's - 11 October 2026

- Thirteen block types followed their content with no way to get a taller box, and the map sized itself by a setting of its own. A block whose type follows its content can be given its own height in the Placement area of the element menu (`height: { mode: "own", align? }`, one additive field): it takes the dragged height and places its content at the top, in the middle, at the bottom or stretched; `followsContent` in push-model.js reads the field, so the push pass, the fit, the settle and the drag stop follow it, and base.css lays the box out as a one-row grid. Stretch is offered where the height is used: stats, countdown and quote, and the calendar in its plain list and card views, where the rows and cards spread. Fields in SCHEMA.md, the mechanics in ARCHITECTURE.md.
- The map's box is its frame: the height setting went, the iframe fills what the frame leaves over the «Larger map» line, and on the phone the map takes the frame's height like the other blocks with a box of their own. The page migration 4 → 5 (`liftMapHeights`) gives a stored map's frame the height it showed and moves the blocks below as a fit would; the block's 1 → 2 migration drops the field, template payloads are lifted on insertion, and the example pages are lifted to schemaVersion 5. The preset test required version 1 on every preset block; it accepts any version from 1.
- Test finding: a calendar made narrower until it took its phone layout grew taller, and the section grew with it instead of letting the block lie over the section below. A section's own height is the owner's: nothing in the editor raises it (`fitMovesAll` returns the moves alone, the settle and the gestures no longer carry a section height, `ownHeightPx` and the `minHeight` fields of `urd-move`, `urd-frames` and `urd-move-block-section` are gone), and the push pass holds the section line in the preview (`_urdPushPreview`) while raising it on the published page, where the visitor's content has to fit. A section with no height of its own follows its blocks as before. ADR-0024 decision 2 and ADR-0025 decisions 5 and 8 are amended.
- Test finding: a seam of the section's background showed between the selection outline and a text box at the scaled preview's fractional pixel edges. The outline, single and multiple, overlaps the box edge by a pixel.
- ADR-0024 and ADR-0025 had grown five dated addenda of build details and sequences of events. Both are rewritten as standing decisions with the amendments in place and the mechanics moved to ARCHITECTURE.md («Desktop sizing: the push pass, the fit and the gestures») and SCHEMA.md; the earlier texts are kept under `docs/adr/amended/`, and AGENTS.md carries the rule (set 11 October 2026).
- BACKLOG: the huskeliste is brought up to date after the push of 0.7.19.19-29p.

### 0.7.19.19-29p - Push preparation: the calendar's sets, registry and picker reviewed - 11 October 2026

- Review finding: the registry's web page reached the card's «More about» link and the structured data with no guard but the schema's pattern, which the engine never enforces. `calendarEntry` keeps the link only as an http(s) address (an anchored pattern), with a test.
- Review finding: the «missing calendar» note stood under a block whose only source row was still empty, since the note tested the raw count. It shows only when a source is a reference to a calendar the registry lacks.
- Review finding: an event of a calendar kept out of the filter lost the words before «Category:» from its title as well as its chip. The title stays whole.
- Review finding: the phone-design class on the block was set only in the branch that draws events, so an empty redraw kept a stale one. It is set before the branches.
- Review finding: the pictures script left its temporary profile behind when the browser could not start, and crashed without a page target. The browser's error is awaited and the target guarded.
- Review finding: the calendar guide named the switcher's settings «Behind Week» and «Behind Month» while the panel says «Week design» and «Month design». The guide follows the panel.
- Review finding: the 0.7.19.29 entry logged the preview bridge's source check, which 0.7.19.22 made. Dropped there.
- Review finding: the design picker's dialog was two rems wider and taller than the window (no border-box), so the card sat off centre and lost its right and bottom edges on a small window. The dialog is sized with border-box.
- Review finding: opened from the block palette, the picker marked Plain as the chosen design although no block was selected, beside the highlighted tile. Add mode has no current design, and the badge on a selected block's design reads «In use».
- Review finding: the picker sent the page to its preview twice at open, and a close that was not a cancel (a second Escape the browser forces) hid the dialog while the picker stayed mounted, so «Design» did not reopen it. The effect sends once, and the dialog's close event leaves the picker.
- Review finding: four comments in App.svelte described code the picker had replaced, the description setting's tip sat on a wrapper around its choice, and the block's reference row painted a calendar without a colour as transparent where the Site panel shows the accent. The comments are gone, the tip is the choice's own, and both swatches use the same helper.
- Mechanical checks: no em dashes, the test batches' checkbox syntax, the stylesheet stamp current in the eight shells, the version fields consistent, and the committed bundle equal to a fresh build.

### 0.7.19.29 - The site's calendars - 10 October 2026

- Every calendar block carried its own sources, so the same calendar could be named and coloured differently from block to block, and «Calendars on the site» only scanned the other blocks for addresses. The site registers its calendars once in `site.calendars` (site.json, additive; schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit), each with an address, a name, a description, four colours (the main colour, the text on it, the event box and the chip), its own web page, an organizer and two switches (can be subscribed to, in the calendar filter), and a block picks one as the source `{ "calendar": "<id>" }`. The bare address and `{ url, name, color }` forms stay valid.
- The engine resolves the references at draw time (pure `resolveSources` and `calendarEntry` in ics.js; a reference to a calendar not in the registry is dropped, the same calendar twice is one source, and a block whose sources resolve to nothing shows the sample data with a note in the preview). A calendar kept out of the filter gives its events no category, so they carry no chip and the calendar is in no chip row or legend; a calendar that cannot be subscribed to is left out of the subscribe row and the card's «whole calendar» links; the card names the organizer, shows the calendar's description and links «More about {name}»; search engines get the organizer with the calendar's page (`eventJsonLd`). The extra colours are written as `--urd-cal-color-text`, `--urd-cal-color-bg` and `--urd-cal-chip-color` on the event's box and chip, which base.css reads before its derived colours.
- Test finding 10 October 2026: a source's name and colour (0.7.13.13) never reached the events of a real feed, since `expandEvents` built every occurrence from a fixed list of fields. The occurrence carries its source, and the name, the colour and the registry's words follow it.
- The Site panel gets the group «Calendars» (the canvas «Urd kalenderregister», proposal B chosen 10 October 2026): «New calendar» at the top, a list of closed rows (colour, name, address), one row open at a time with the sections Basics, Colours (four swatches on one row) and Shown to visitors, and move and «Remove calendar» at its foot. The body slides between its heights with a grid row from `0fr` to `1fr`, the closed bodies are `inert`, the list has `overflow-anchor: none` so the panel does not jump with the sliding rows, and the new calendar opens with the caret in its name.
- In a block's Sources, a reference is a read-only row with the calendar's colour and name, the site's calendars stand as a checkbox list, «Save in the site's calendars» moves an own address into the registry and makes the row a reference, and «Edit the site's calendars» opens the Site panel with the group open. The block's source helpers snapshot the state before writing it back, since a state proxy cannot be posted to the preview.
- The calendar guide in English and Norwegian describes the registry, and the admin dictionaries in five languages and the site dictionaries in six get the keys.
- Along the way: the Svelte compiler's style pruning crawled for over ten minutes on the group's first styles, which used `+` and `>` combinators and a universal selector that the rest of the editor never uses; the rules are plain class selectors and the editor builds in three minutes as before.

### 0.7.19.22 - The design picker - 8 October 2026

- The calendar designs stood as a fold of schematic thumbnails in the Style tab, the block menu and the block palette, with no room for the count. The design picker (`editor/src/lib/DesignPicker.svelte`) opens over the editor from all three places: every design with a picture, grouped by family (`family` in `CAL_DESIGNS`, `CAL_FAMILIES`, pure `calDesignFamilies`), a search on the name, a filter on the view, the chosen design marked, and the marked design drawn live in a preview iframe of its own in the site's theme and language before «Use this design» or «Add calendar». The three folds and the menu's own picker are removed.
- The pictures are the engine's own drawings of each design with the sample events, 34 WebP files under `template/admin/designs/` made by the new `scripts/design-pictures.mjs` (a headless browser against the local server; the site's header, strip and scroll-top button kept out). They lie outside `admin/assets/`, which the editor build empties. A design without a picture shows its schematic thumbnail, and a test holds that every design has a family and a picture.
- The picker is a native modal dialog (ADR-0011), so it stands centred whatever panel it was opened from, and its preview's height follows the drawn block through the preview window's own ResizeObserver, with the site's header, announcement strip and footer hidden in that iframe.
- The preview bridge heard every message on the window; it now hears its own iframe alone (`previewBridge.js`), so the picker's iframe never reaches the editor's handlers.
- SCHEMA.md describes the family and the picker, the calendar guide in English and Norwegian describes choosing a design, and the five admin dictionaries get the picker's keys.

### 0.7.19.21 - The phone design, and «Add to calendar» switchable - 8 October 2026

- Table and Heatmap could not be read on a phone (four squeezed columns; a year grid twelve screens tall). Every design declares how it behaves on a phone (`phone` in `CAL_DESIGNS`: fits, flows or switch, `CAL_PHONE_MODES`), and a design that switches draws the shared phone design there: one stacked agenda in the set's colours and type, as drawn on the canvas (V29 to V37), with the «Upcoming» heading, a row per event and the fold (`renderPhoneAgenda` in blocks/calendar.js, `.urd-cal-pa-*` in base.css). `phoneDesign: false` shows the design as it is (pure `calPhoneDesign`), and the «Phone design» switch is shown only on a design that switches.
- The poster wall drew two cramped columns on a phone, with the first poster spanning both. It and the picture cards flow into one column there, whatever the columns option says.
- «Add to calendar» stood in every event's card with no way to leave it out. The setting `showAdd` (`false` leaves it out) with the switch «Show «Add to calendar»» under Content, on by default.
- SCHEMA.md describes `phone`, `phoneDesign` and `showAdd`; the calendar guide in English and Norwegian describes the phone design and the switch; the five admin dictionaries get the two settings' keys.

### 0.7.19.20 - The description in the rows or only in the card - 8 October 2026

- Programme booklet and Split card each had a per-design toggle for the description, and the other designs with room for an excerpt had none. The block setting `description` (`rows` or `card`, pure `calDescription`, which reads the legacy `options.description: false` as `card`) says whether the description stands in the event's own row or only in the card that opens at a press; the two per-design toggles leave `CAL_OPTIONS`, and the editor shows the setting only on designs that draw an excerpt (`excerpt` in the design list, pure `calHasExcerpt`).

### 0.7.19.19 - The card, the switcher's views and the finding tools per design set - 8 October 2026

- The event card measured its colours from the pressed event (`getComputedStyle` in `dressDialog`), which read on some designs and not on others. Every design belongs to one of ten colour sets (`set` in `CAL_DESIGNS`, `CAL_SETS` in calendar-designs.js, drawn on the canvas page «Bygges nå: de 34 designene»); base.css defines the set variables under `urd-cal-set-<id>` and draws the card from them, and the dialog is dressed by copying the host's set class and colour slots alone. A test holds every design to a set and the card rules to no measured variable.
- The switcher's Week and Month were the plain week strip and month whatever the design. They are drawn in a box of their own inside the block's set (`.urd-cal-swview`, re-themed to the set's variables), and the setting `switcherViews` chooses the design behind each button (Week design and Month design under the switcher toggle; `CAL_SWITCH_WEEKS`, `CAL_SWITCH_MONTHS`, pure `calSwitcherViews`, the ApeironLF month as the default on the Ap sets).
- The switcher's buttons, the chips, the search field, the «nothing matches» line and the «Earlier» fold had one neutral look. They read the set variables, with per-set form rules (the gold frame on the Ap sets, squares on poster, tiles on board, pinned notes on cork, glass on the glass sets), and `toolsSize` (`s` or `l`) sizes them with the week's and month's arrows.
- The place filter is removed and a stored `showPlaces` ignored, since the search finds a place (decided 8 October 2026). The chip row is the calendars and is drawn only with `showCategories: true` (an older block without the key loses the row it got by default); its label says calendar filter.
- On a phone the switcher and the search stand on one row, with the search behind a button that opens the field (`.urd-cal-searchbtn`).
- In the preview a calendar without sources stood clipped to its frame. The sample data follows the content like a feed (render.js and calendar.js no longer treat the sample apart), so the block grows and pushes what stands below.
- Test finding 8 October 2026: the edge stripe setting did nothing in the week plan and the day plan, whose boxes drew an edge of their own, and the week strip behind the switcher's button lost it to the set's border rule. The plans' boxes and pills and the layers' bars take their edge from the stripe rule (`stripe: true` on Week plan and Day plan keeps their look), and the stripe wins over the set's borders in the switcher's week strip.
- Test finding 8 October 2026: Month with side panel drew a key of the calendars with the filter off and the sample calendars all in one colour. The key is drawn only with the calendar filter on and a calendar colour set, and the three sample calendars carry colours of their own on their events (`DEMO_COLORS`).
- Test finding 8 October 2026: «Show all N» could not be opened in the editor, since its rewritable words stopped the press. The fold follows the block's two-step model: the press that selects the block leaves it, the next press opens or closes it and goes into the words, and the presses after that edit the words until they lose focus.
- A choice with more than three options or long labels stacked badly in the panel (Choice.svelte stacks them). SCHEMA.md describes the sets, `switcherViews`, `toolsSize`, `description` and the retired `showPlaces`; the calendar guide and the user guide in English and Norwegian describe the settings; the admin and site dictionaries lose the place filter's keys and gain the new settings' keys in all six languages.

### 0.7.0.40-43p - Push preparation: the test rounds restored, and the calendar's words reviewed - 7 October 2026

- Independent review of the span by three fresh agents (the calendar renderers with the style sheet, the pure modules with the dictionaries, and the editor with the documents), after the review of 0.7.0.43 alone at its commit. Deviation from the ritual: 0.7.0.40 to 0.7.0.42 went out as docs-only pushes on 6 October without a push entry, so this entry names them in its span.
- TESTRUNDER at HEAD was a merge that lost both sides: the web edit of 6 October (7dff4ad) struck every batch older than 0.7.13.9, and 0.7.0.40 merged and struck within the newer ones, but the merge commit e45c01d brought 686 struck items back and dropped 36 items 0.7.0.40 had rewritten, so 901 items stood where neither side had them (found by the document review, which also found the entry's «818 items remain» false at HEAD). The two edits combine without conflict, since the web edit struck whole batches only: the file is 0.7.0.40's version cut after batch 0.7.13.9, with the two new batches on top, 217 items.
- The event card in the editor closed on a press on the text toolbar, the colour picker or a menu, since they lie outside the card, so Bold on the card's words removed the card instead (found by reading, confirmed with a press on Bold). A press on them leaves the card open.
- A click into the words of «Show all» or «Earlier» opened or closed the fold on every click, since the summary's own activation is not stopped by a stopped event. The words stop it as they stop a link.
- The 12-hour clock and the percentage of a language Intl does not know followed the visitor's browser rather than the site (`formatClock(18, 0, true, 'xx')` gave «6:00 PM» on an English runtime). An unknown tag is Bokmål, as `calWeekStart` already read it.
- The admin hints of an empty announcement were prepared on the published page too, holding a detached node and a promise reaction per blank text. They are prepared in the editor only.
- A zero-width space stood as a literal character inside a regex in `serialize`, and a doc comment in App.svelte sat above the wrong function after the announcement's fields were inserted under it; both are written as they read. Five comments wrapped mid-sentence are reflowed, the 0.7.0.43 entry is cut to symptom and action, and SCHEMA.md names `readWhole` and the admin hints.
- Closed without a change: `calBlankHtml` reads a lone image as blank (the text toolbar puts no images in these words); `calHasTextOverrides` sees only the current design's texts (as before); the guard test's regexes miss forms no renderer uses; the Northern Sami hint for the announcement's text keeps the earlier wording (a machine draft, for the translation round); «Undo the last publish» after an update is the v0.9 item from 0.7.0.43.

### 0.7.0.43 - The test site and local server always load the newest engine - 7 October 2026

- The test site deployed from the monorepo could stay blank after a push until the browser's cache was emptied, and the admin preview there ran an old engine under a new editor: the engine files were cached for a year while changing under the same folder. The monorepo's `template/_headers` revalidates the engine (`Cache-Control: no-cache`); a local `wrangler pages dev` follows the same rule, and `dev-server.py` is unchanged (ADR-0013 addendum).
- The release writes the template's own rule back: `release.yml` runs the new `scripts/release-headers.mjs` on its copy of `_headers`, which swaps in the immutable engine block byte for byte as released, and stops the run before the commit unless the copy's engine rule is the immutable one. `tests/release-headers.test.mjs` pins the result to the template's released blob.
- `scripts/check-release.mjs` accepts only the release workflow of `main` or of the synced tag.
- ADR-0013 gets an addendum (immutable only in a release), and ARCHITECTURE.md, the development document in English and Norwegian and AGENTS.md's release paragraph describe the swap.
- BACKLOG: the URGENT item and the v0.9 item on an emptied browser cache are delivered. Two v0.9 items are added under the updater hardening: a page that heals itself after an update on released sites (a boot guard, with the research behind this commit), and «Undo the last publish» reverting an Urd update committed after the publish.

### 0.7.19.18 - All calendar texts follow the site language and can be edited - 7 October 2026

- About 40 visible words in the 34 built calendar designs bypassed the rewritable texts, every text with a number among them. They are rewritable texts whose values stand as islands that a rewrite keeps as `{n}`, `{date}` and the like, stored per plural form (`CAL_TEXTS`, `CAL_PLURAL_TEXTS` and `CAL_TEXT_PARAMS` in calendar-designs.js, `ui.tx` in calendar.js; SCHEMA.md).
- Dates were joined in Norwegian order, the 12-hour clock wrote `am`/`pm` and shares `45 %` in every language, and the year wheel's `toUpperCase()` wrote Turkish İ wrong. Dates go through dictionary templates, the clock, hours and shares through Intl (`formatClock`, `formatHour` and `formatPercent` in calendar-format.js), and the wheel's capitals through CSS.
- The countdown units read «1 dager», a picked day that was not today said «N i dag», an empty day or month said «No upcoming events», and «I dag!» could show for an event tomorrow at 01:00. The units have plural forms, the picked day and the empty day and month have words of their own, and the countdown counts calendar days (`daysBetween`).
- Content a design ships with (the announcement's label, the empty state's pill, the regular event's labels) came back with its default when emptied, and an emptied text holding `<br>` drew an empty line. An emptied content line is stored as `false`, left out on the page and in the Clean view, and stands as a faint hint in the editor (`CAL_CONTENT_TEXTS`, `ui.line`, `ui.group`); any other blank text shows its default.
- The announcement's default title and text were editor instructions a visitor saw, and the preview's sample events followed the admin language. The announcement shows hints in the admin language in the editor only and is not drawn without words of its own, a reset of the texts keeps it (`calResetTexts`), and the sample events follow the site language.
- Test finding 7 October 2026: pressing the words of the alert band in the preview followed its link and left the page, as did every link with rewritable words. The words stop the link while they are edited (`editLink`).
- The announcement could only be written on the note, and the event card could not be opened in the editor. The calendar's menu has Heading and Text fields under Announcement, and a press on an event of the selected calendar opens its card, with the card above the selected block and a press inside it keeping the selection (preview-edit.js, base.css).
- A long announcement made its note as tall as its text. The text stops after four lines, six in the Regular event's aside, and a cut text gets «Read it all», which opens the announcement in full in a card like an event's (`ui.notice`, `showNoticeDialog`); in the editor a press on the cut text of the selected calendar opens it.
- The cut was never found in testing, since Chrome leaves the clamped lines out of `scrollHeight`. The text is measured without the clamp instead.
- A test holds the renderers to no clock halves, capitals or dictionary words of their own. BACKLOG moves the theme words, «Word choice» and the switch for rewriting calendar data to 0.7.19.23, and the calendar guide in English and Norwegian describes the words, the announcement's fields and the cards.

### 0.7.0.42 - The READMEs brought up to date - 6 October 2026

- The pronunciation in the English and Turkish READMEs ran to a paragraph on the vowel, the «rd» and the word's history. It is the phonetic form and a rough spelling, «*Urd* [ʉːɖ], roughly **oord**», and the English heading's misspelling «Pronounciation» is corrected.
- The READMEs said the site can be served by any static host, GitHub Pages included, though the sign-in and the publishing from `/admin` run on Cloudflare Pages Functions. All five say what works today and that any static host is planned for v0.9, which BACKLOG, ROADMAP and VEIKART now carry as a v0.9 item.
- The calendar was described as a feed with four views; it reads five views and 34 designs with an event card, search and filters. The English, Turkish and Sami READMEs linked the Norwegian roadmap from their status line; they link the English one.
- The language notes said the documents are in Norwegian and, in the Norwegian README, that Norwegian is the working language. They say English is the canonical language and that the change log, the backlog and the test checklist hold older entries in Norwegian. The Nynorsk, Turkish and Sami tables gain the calendar and photo guides, and the Turkish and Sami ones link the English vision, roadmap and development documents.
- The Northern Sami README called Urd's files unreadable («lohkanmeahttun»), static «stáhtalaš» (governmental), the gap analysis a shoe analysis («gapme») and the change log «in the name of» each push, and copied Norwegian «Ingen server» word for word (found by reading it through). Those and a handful of inflections are corrected; the mythology paragraph and three terms are left for a native speaker, under the machine-translation note.

### 0.7.0.41 - The later phases back in BACKLOG - 6 October 2026

- BACKLOG had no v0.8, v0.9, v1.0 or after-1.0 items: the commit 0.7.21.1 (3 October 2026) removed the sections «Til v0.8 og senere faser», «Moderniser til native/CSS» and «Etter v1.0 (horisont)», 59 lines, along with the stages it rewrote, and its entry did not say so (found 6 October 2026). The three sections are restored word for word from the version before that commit, between the v0.7 milestone and «Liten huskeliste»; none of their items has been built since.

### 0.7.0.40 - The test rounds reviewed and merged - 6 October 2026

- TESTRUNDER held 996 open items, many repeated across batches or describing features since replaced. The calendar batches from 0.7.18.4 to 0.7.19.17p are one batch with one item per design and the shared checks once, the element menu and the sizing model are one batch each, and items replaced by later changes are removed or rewritten (the old settings panels, the design dropdown, the site-level scaling, «My screen», the plugins that became core blocks, among others). The file's introduction says an item may also go when a later change replaced it.

### 0.7.20.4 - The search in the element menu - 4 October 2026

- A setting deep in a closed group could only be found by opening groups, and the calendar's menu had grown by eleven settings since they were built. A search field in the floating menu's head and over the menu in Properties narrows the menu to the settings whose label or tooltip holds every word, with their groups open (`menu-search.js`, `matchWords` in palette-search.js, tests/menu-search.test.mjs).
- The search reads the menu as it is drawn, so every block type has it, and draws its own copy of the areas, so emptying the field brings back the groups and the tab as they were left. The wide menu keeps its columns, each saying when nothing in it matches, and the narrow one shows its areas under each other.
- Esc empties the field, another block or a closed menu empties the search, and a word typed closes the design picker.
- The field picker in «Text fields» has no words of its own and would have been hidden from the size and colour it sets. A control without words shows whenever its group does.
- The user guide in English and Norwegian describes the element menu and its search.

### 0.7.21.4 - The block follows its own width - 4 October 2026

- A block with columns or cards laid itself out by the window: a narrow calendar on a wide page kept its wide layout, and the five calendar designs that change at 600 px (Bento, Month with side panel, Year wheel, Split card, Regular event) changed by the window only. Calendar, collection, gallery, product cards, statistic, timeline and table are now size containers (`FOLLOWS_WIDTH` in push-model.js, `urd-block` in base.css), and the calendar's width rules ask the block, with the window's media query kept beside them for a browser without container queries (a test holds the two equal).
- The calendar's narrow layouts (the month's dots, Overview on cream, the stacked Week plan) were chosen by the page's phone view. They are chosen by the calendar's own width, below 540 px, and change while the block is dragged (a zero-height ruler observed for the width, drawn again in the next frame; the window decides without container queries).
- Galleries and product cards with a fixed column count kept it in a narrow block. They take at most two columns below 540 px, product cards one below 320 px, and the mosaic gets a wall of its own for the narrow count.
- The alternating timeline lays its line along the left edge below 480 px, a table tightens its cells below 420 px, and a figure shrinks below 220 px. Collection cards already filled their box.
- ADR-0025 gets an addendum on how the block's own width is built, and the user guide and the calendar guide say that these blocks follow their own width, in English and Norwegian.

### 0.7.21.2.2 - A drag never stretches a section - 4 October 2026

- Test finding 4 October 2026: a block dragged towards the bottom of its section stretched the section instead of lying over the edge, the symptom of 0.7.0.22 back with a new cause: the box the owner sees was taller than the frame the section line reads, by a frame never fitted to its content (a page saved before 0.7.21.2, a collection's entries, a feed) or by the editor's own parts. When a gesture begins, the frames under it are settled to the boxes the push pass draws, the blocks below and a section's own height taking what the pass drew, so the section line holds still and a block dragged past the edge lies over it (`settleFrames` in preview-edit.js, `fitMovesAll` in push-model.js; ADR-0025 addendum).
- The settle covers a drag, a set dragged by a member or by the selection toolbar's grip, a drop in another section, the arrow keys, align and distribute; it is one undo step with the placement, and a gesture that changes nothing puts it back.
- The editor's own parts (the image adders, «+ Product», the placeholders of empty fields) counted as growth in the editor alone, so the front page's collection stood 174 px taller than on the published page and raised its section there. They are drawn but never push (`EDITOR_ONLY` and `visitorHeights` in render.js, held equal to base.css by a test).
- A resize left its block out of the section's height while it was dragged, so content that grew under the drag raised the section only on release; a group drag posted each member's frame as it was when the drag began, which reverted a fit that landed during it (both found by the review of the plan). The resized block counts while it is dragged, and each member is posted as it stands.
- A field just typed into in a collection card kept the placeholder's class until a redraw, so Clean view hid the typed words (found by reading). Only an empty placeholder is hidden.
- A fit in a section without a height of its own left the section at the extent it was drawn with, so a taller frame hung below it until the next redraw (found by reading). The design height is written again from the frames (`syncSectionHeight` in render.js).
- ADR-0025 gets a second addendum, and SCHEMA.md's `frames` paragraph describes the settle.

### 0.7.21.2 - The height that follows the content, for fourteen block types - 4 October 2026

- Fourteen block types stood at the top of a frame taller than their content, an outline with air under it. Calendar, Collection, Product cards, Basket, Checkout, Countdown, FAQ, Form, Quote, Share buttons, Statistic, Table, Timeline and Audio are drawn at their content's height, also below the frame, and the blocks below keep their places (`FOLLOWS_CONTENT` in push-model.js, the push pass in render.js).
- Only the calendar had its frame fitted, after a change of its settings. Every block that follows its content is fitted after an edit of its settings, of its words in the preview or of its width, and when it is placed; the calendar's `growOnly` is gone.
- The calendar's fit wrote the taller height alone, so the blocks the push had moved below it jumped back up over it (found by reading the fit against the push pass). A taller fit moves them, and a section's own height in px, as far as the push pass drew them, in the undo step of the edit (`fitMoves`, held by tests/push-model.test.mjs; ADR-0025 addendum).
- The fit comes back without drawing the section again (`urd-frames`), so a caret in the block's text stays where it is, and it measures the block at rest as the Clean view shows it: an open FAQ answer or calendar fold, the image adders and the placeholders of empty fields are left out (`restingHeight` in render.js).
- A drag of the lower corner changed the height of any block. On a block that follows its content the corner changes the width only: a pull up or down stops at the content's edge with a mark, «The height follows the content», and a pull straight up or down changes nothing; the handle's tooltip and cursor say so.
- The audio block's box stood at the frame's height with the player at its top; it follows the player. No rule stretched the player itself.
- Eight blocks kept a height net of their own that the push pass overwrote, and the FAQ's fixed the block's height on a phone when an answer opened (found by reading). The nets are gone from Quote, Timeline, Table, Statistic, Checkout, Collection, Product cards and FAQ.
- An open basket drawer counted as the Basket block's content and pushed the blocks below down by a window's height (found by reading). A dialog never counts towards a block's height.
- SCHEMA.md, the user guide and the calendar guide in English and Norwegian describe the height that follows the content; AGENTS.md lists push-model among the modules the editor bundles; BACKLOG links the Design canvas «Urd calendar designs» and counts its stylised designs in six families, not five.

### 0.7.0.36-0.7.19.17p - Push preparation: the calendar hardened against real feeds, clocks and colours - 4 October 2026

- Independent review of the span by seven fresh agents, each with its own field: the parser and the pure calendar modules, the calendar block, the 34 designs, the style sheet, the editor, the contracts and documents, and the commits outside the calendar. Deviation from the ritual: all seven stopped on a usage limit before reporting and were resumed; the style sheet review stopped a second time and was started anew on another model.
- Data loss: two texts rewritten in one calendar overwrote each other, since each posted the props it was drawn with and the editor stores what it is sent without drawing again (found by reading, confirmed in the preview with two edits). The texts rewritten since the block was drawn are kept together.
- The parser: the picture link was found with a regex that ran in quadratic time, so a 105 KB description took 1.8 s per occurrence (measured); it is a linear scan of the addresses. `BYMONTH` in feed order dropped occurrences and miscounted `COUNT`; `RDATE` dates ignored their `RECURRENCE-ID` overrides; a rule that began years ago never reached the window, since the guard counted from the start; and `INTERVAL=Infinity`, an empty `GEO` part, a PDF in `ATTACH`, «Pamela» as a sign-up word, a sign-up line holding a meeting link, `BYMONTHDAY=-1`, `BYDAY=+1MO` and a daily rule with `BYDAY` all gave wrong events. Each is fixed with a test in tests/calendar.test.mjs.
- Days and clocks: an all-day event's length was counted in milliseconds, so a weekend over a change of clock became one day and its export ended where it began; the JSON-LD end of an all-day event was a day early; all-day events were moved to the site's time zone and could land on the day before; and `shiftToZone` read the visitor's offset at the wrong moment and was an hour off near the visitor's own change of clock (52 and 76 wrong half hours in a year, measured in two zones). Lengths are counted in calendar days, dates are left on their day, and the offset is read at the shifted moment, held by a test over four weeks in three zones.
- The designs: Week plan and Calendar layers stepped 7 × 24 hours and drifted to a Sunday 23:00 across a change of clock; bars and day filters counted 24-hour days, so an all-day event was missing on its last day; an event past midnight vanished from the plan; a cancelled next event struck and dimmed the «Later» rows in four designs; the timeline opened only from its dot; the hour axis ignored the 12-hour clock; the week number was wrong for a Sunday week; dates and Dark glass's clock were written in Norwegian on English and Turkish sites («5. Oct», «t» for hours). Each design uses calendar days, its own words and the site's date form (found by the design review, measured under Europe/Oslo).
- The block: an open card stayed over a calendar drawn again; a failed module or a feed that threw left the loading bars standing; a programme link to another site pulled the editor's preview away in the Clean view; the month's today and the bento's today were on the visitor's clock; the card had no name for a screen reader, an event opened with Enter only, and the search pulled the focus back from where the visitor had moved it. All fixed in blocks/calendar.js.
- The style sheet: the dark agenda and List on navy drew light text on light ground in the shared parts (the view switcher, the search field, the switcher's month), measured at 1.1:1 to 1.8:1, now 13:1 to 20:1; a still band could not be scrolled, since its rule stood before the track's; the dark agenda printed light on white; the text on the calendar's accent and on an event's calendar colour kept the theme's pair, 1.4:1 on a yellow accent (the new `inkOn` in theme.js picks the ink that stands out more); the chapters and split footers kept two columns on a phone; booklet, picture and grid columns overflowed at phone width.
- The editor: the frame fit added an undo step of its own, a late answer wrote a phone-width height into the desktop frame or undid a drag, and every reported height drew the section again; the plain design kept a week view it cannot show; the design picker outlived its menu; the «changed» dot disagreed with the counts a new calendar starts with. Each answer now carries the number of the fit it answers, and only the latest counts.
- Outside the calendar: an own icon from an animated file went into the draft at full size, and above 4 MB failed without a word; a file above the cap was read whole to find out it moves (its beginning is enough); a divider's theme colour was read inside the section's own theme and vanished in an inverse section.
- Documents: neither the calendar guide nor the setup guide said that every calendar service but Google needs its host in `ICS_HOSTS`, so the services the guide lists would have shown nothing; both say so now. SCHEMA.md said both that sign-up shows when omitted and only when true; help texts and the source placeholder described the editor before its groups; the first seven calendar stages shared one «Done when»; fifteen CHANGELOG bullets ran far past two sentences; and the comments in the new modules broke sentences across lines. All corrected; a script check confirmed the rewrapping moved no code and no word.
- The fix round's own regression: the new failure path in blocks/calendar.js lacked a parenthesis (`}.catch`), which `node --check` accepted because it read the file as a script, and the probe in the browser found. tests/engine-syntax.test.mjs parses every engine file as a module; it fails on the same mistake put back.
- Review notes, left for later in BACKLOG: the Year wheel's top month turns only the ring; `WKST` is ignored; a countdown's timer lives up to a minute after a redraw; the printed list keeps the screen's block heights; a touch cannot pause the band; Glass and Dark glass read at about 4:1 by default; and the designs with a fixed 600 px rule move to container queries in 0.7.21.4.

### 0.7.19.17 - The calendar's designs in the palette and the block menu, a template per view, and its texts in three more languages - 4 October 2026

- The palette and the block menu offered five calendar views and none of the designs. The Blocks panel shows «Designs» under Calendar with a thumbnail of each of the 34, the block search finds a design by its name, and the «+ New block» menu lists the designs beside the views (`variants` in blocks/calendar.js). A new calendar's frame is fitted to its design when it is placed.
- «What is on» existed as a list only. Four more section templates: cards, month, week and next.
- The calendar's texts were missing outside the three core languages: 276 admin texts in Nynorsk, 276 admin and 79 visitor texts in the Swedish pack, and 224 admin and 79 visitor texts in Northern Sami, where 52 admin texts are left to fall back to bokmål. None of them is reviewed by a native speaker.
- The calendar guide gets a «Designs» section, and the user guide's calendar paragraph is rewritten, in English and Norwegian.
- Test finding 4 October 2026: a press on an event scrolled the page to its top. The card took its first place at the top of the page before it opened; it takes it in the part of the page that is in view.
- Test finding: in a week strip the time and the title ran out of the event's box. The two had no space between them to break at, and a narrow strip kept seven columns. A space stands between them, a long word breaks, and a strip narrower than 540 px lays its days under each other (a container query in base.css).

### 0.7.19.16 - The calendar reads more of a feed, tells a meeting and a sign-up apart from a link, and gets a guide of its own - 4 October 2026

- The parser missed rules and fields real feeds use. It reads `BYSETPOS` («the last Thursday of the month»), `BYMONTH` and `RDATE`, the meeting link from `CONFERENCE` and the fields Google and Microsoft write it in, `GEO` and the HTML description of `X-ALT-DESC`, each with a fixture in tests/calendar.test.mjs.
- A place with coordinates opens the point itself in OpenStreetMap and Apple Maps (`mapSearchUrl` in map-links.js), and an event with coordinates and no place in words gets «Show on the map» in its card.
- A feed's HTML description was set into the page and cleaned afterwards. It is built from an allowlist of harmless tags with no attribute carried over (`safeHtmlFragment` in sanitize.js), so a picture, a style, a form or embedded content in a feed is left out.
- The first link of a description became «Sign up» whatever it led to, also a picture or a meeting, and the card showed the button with the switch off. The sign-up is the link on a line that names signing up, registering or tickets, else the event's own address (`signupLinkOf` in ics.js), never a picture or a meeting, and the card follows «Show «Sign up» buttons».
- Meeting links were known for six services. Proton Meet, kMeet and Element Call are added, and a site lists the hosts of its own servers under «Own meeting addresses» in the Site panel (`site.meetingHosts`, SCHEMA.md; the new meeting-links.js). «Join» and «Sign up» show the host they lead to as their tooltip.
- A Nextcloud calendar's share link opens a web page, not the calendar file. Pasted as a source, the link from the share dialog and the public address without `?export` are turned into the address of the iCal file (`normalizeSourceUrl` in ics.js).
- New guide for site owners, docs/languages/calendar-guide/ in English and Norwegian: the calendar services that give a public iCal address and where to find it, what is read from an event, meeting links, sign-up, places and maps, finding events, times, privacy and what the block cannot do. It is linked from the user guide and the README tables.

### 0.7.19.15 - Finding events in the calendar: a search, a place filter, earlier events, and the feed's categories - 4 October 2026

- A visitor could narrow a calendar by category only. Three switches on the block, each off until set: «Show search field» (the words are looked for in titles, places and descriptions, `matchesSearch` in ics.js), «Show place filter» (a button per venue, the place up to its first comma) and «Show earlier events» (the last 90 days' events that are over, folded under «Earlier» with their count, in the views that count out what is coming). The three narrow together with the category, and a search or filter that leaves nothing says so.
- A feed's own `CATEGORIES` were ignored. They are read, and the first is the event's category when its calendar has no name, before the «Category: Title» convention.
- The filters and the «Earlier» fold have one neutral look in every design; drawing them per design is added to the design round, 0.7.19.19.

### 0.7.0.39 - The event card leaves the page alone, and places link to a map service the site chooses - 4 October 2026

- Test finding 3 October 2026: with an event's card open the page could not be scrolled, and the background moved as the scrollbar went. The card opens as a dialog that is not modal: the page scrolls under it, Escape, the close button and a press outside close it, and one card is open at a time.
- Test finding: the card followed the window while the page scrolled. It is placed on the page, over its calendar and under the navigation bar, and scrolls away with it.
- Test finding: OpenStreetMap found nothing for a place written as a venue's name before its address. Each map service is given the search it finds (`mapQuery` in the new map-links.js): the venue's name is left out for the services that know addresses only, and the country for Norgeskart.
- The map a place opens in is a choice in the Site panel, «Map service» (`site.mapService`, SCHEMA.md): OpenStreetMap as before, DuckDuckGo Maps, Brave Maps, HERE WeGo, Google Maps, Apple Maps, Norgeskart and FINN kart. Each choice carries a note in the list on who makes it, where they are from and what it does with a search; the admin dropdown takes a note per option for it.

### 0.7.0.38 - A host compared exactly in the photo source test - 3 October 2026

- CodeQL alert 15 (incomplete URL substring sanitization): the upstream stub in tests/photo-source.test.mjs picked its answer by looking for the host's name anywhere in the address. It parses the address and compares the host exactly.

### 0.7.19.14 - The calendar's events as structured data - 3 October 2026

- Search engines got no machine-readable events from a calendar. On the published page a calendar with a feed writes the events it shows into `<head>` as schema.org `Event` JSON-LD, one script per block: name, start and end, status, attendance mode, the place or the meeting link, description, picture, address and organiser (`eventJsonLd` in ics.js). Never in the preview and never from the sample data.
- «Tell search engines about the events» on the block switches it off (`structuredData`, SCHEMA.md).
- The stage's criterion rests on the Schema Markup Validator, with Google's Rich Results Test as a second check.

### 0.7.19.13 - The calendar's dates as time elements, day grids on the keyboard, and a printed list - 3 October 2026

- Dates and times were plain text. Every date and time that belongs to an event or a day is a `<time datetime>` in all 34 designs: the day for a date, the moment itself for a clock time (`dateTimeAttr` in calendar-format.js, through the field helper's `when`).
- A month had a tab stop per event and its days could not be reached, and the heat map had one per day with an event. A day grid is one tab stop: the arrow keys move between the days, Home and End to the ends of the row, PageUp and PageDown to the month or week before and after, and Tab goes through the day's own events (`dayGrid` in blocks/calendar.js). It covers the plain month, Overview on cream, Month with side panel, Week strip, the day picker of Day plan, Heat map and the months on the phone.
- A new week, month or day was not announced. The label that names it is a live region, the category buttons carry `aria-pressed`, and a press on the view switcher or a category puts the focus back on the pressed button after the redraw.
- A printed page showed the design with its buttons. In print the design is hidden and the events are written as a plain list of date, time, title and place.

### 0.7.19.12 - The calendar's loading state and the seven-column designs on the phone - 3 October 2026

- A calendar with a feed was empty until the events arrived, and showed the empty state for a moment in the preview. It now stands in a loading state (`aria-busy`, quiet bars, «Loading the calendar» for a screen reader): at the frame's height on the desktop, and on the phone at the height the calendar last had in the session.
- Week strip, Week plan and Calendar layers drew seven narrow columns on the phone. There the days stand under each other: the strip's days as rows, the plan's days with their events in the order of the clock, and each calendar layer with its events under it and the day written on each.
- The plain month and Overview on cream cut every title in a phone-wide cell. On the phone a day is a button with a dot per event, and a press lists the day's events under the grid (`phoneDays` in blocks/calendar.js); the week and the month of the view switcher get the same layouts.
- The first load of a session on a phone still jumps, since the height is not known until the calendar is drawn; a stored phone height is added to the backlog as 0.7.21.7.

### 0.7.19.11 - A click on a calendar event opens a card with its details, links and add-to-calendar choices - 3 October 2026

- An event could not be opened. A click or Enter on an event opens it in a native dialog laid inside its calendar: the date and time, the place, the calendar it belongs to, the picture, the whole description with its links, the sign-up and «Join» for a meeting link (`findMeetingLink`). The calendar is shaded behind it, the card stays under the navigation bar and inside the calendar's width and height, and opening and closing it never scrolls the page.
- «Add to calendar» opens the ways to do it: this event to Google or as a file (`eventIcs`, `googleEventUrl`), and the whole calendar as a subscription with its iCal address to copy.
- The card takes its ground, type and accent from the event it was opened from, with a contrast check on the words and the button; a card drawn per design is 0.7.19.19.
- A description was drawn as plain text and a place could not be followed. Descriptions keep their addresses as links, cut at a word and never inside an address, and a place leads to the map, in every design.
- In the Clean view the editing layer took the pointer on every block and stopped every link, so an event could not be opened and a calendar's links did nothing. There a click on an event and the calendar's own links are let through, the rewritable words are plain text, and the element menu closes with the handles.
- The sample data carries a description, a picture, a sign-up and a meeting link. The view switcher's «Uka» reads «Uke».
- BACKLOG: stage 0.7.19.19, the design round for the event card and the switcher's week and month per design.

### 0.7.19.10 - Times and status on the calendar - 3 October 2026

- Only an event's start was written. A timed event with an end reads «18:00-21:00» and an event over several days «until 6 Oct», in every design, through the shared `time`, `timeText` and `hasTime` in the block's ui (the pure rules are in the new calendar-format.js, with tests).
- The parser dropped cancelled events. `expandEvents` keeps them as `cancelled`, and the block draws them with a struck title and «Cancelled» where the time stands, without a sign-up; `showCancelled: false` hides them. Occurrences also carry `hasEnd`.
- The clock is 24 hours in every language; `clock: "12"` on the block writes «6:00 pm».
- The week started on Monday everywhere. It follows the site language (`calWeekStart`), with `weekStart` on the block, in the month grids, the week designs and the weekday rows.
- The site has a time zone (`site.timeZone`, in the Site panel, stored only when Intl knows the name): every visitor then sees the calendar's times on that clock, and a visitor in another zone gets a line under the calendar naming it. Without it, the visitor's own zone is named when the feed is kept in another (`X-WR-TIMEZONE`). The example site is set to Europe/Oslo.
- The Year wheel's «The month at the top of the wheel» takes «The current month».
- AGENTS.md lists `calendar-format` among the engine modules the editor bundles.

### 0.7.20.2 - The calendar's settings in groups - 3 October 2026

- The calendar's Content and Style were two long lists. They are collapsible groups that show their value while closed: Sources, View and count, Buttons and filter, When it is empty and Announcement in Content; Settings for the design (with Size), Colours, Edge stripe and Text fields in Style.
- A group that differs from the defaults carries a mark and «Reset this group» under its controls (`calMenu` in App.svelte; `menuGroup` takes the reset).
- In the wide menu's columns a label stood beside its control and wrapped; there it stands over the control.
- «Reset the texts» stays a button of its own, shown when a text is rewritten, and Sources has no reset.

### 0.7.21.1 - The sizing model decided - 3 October 2026

- What a drag of a block's outline means was never decided: thirteen block types stood at the top of a frame with air under them, the map ignored its frame, and the audio block stretched its player. Every block type was measured in the preview in a frame too short, too tall and narrow, and the builders' models read again; the table and the sources are in LAERDOMMER §2 («What a drag means»).
- The model is in ADR-0025: the dragged width for every block, a height that follows the content or is the block's own with a default per block type and a choice per block, a drag that stops at the content, the frame set by the editor after an edit, and «Size» as the explicit scale. ADR-0024 has an addendum for the one point it amends.
- BACKLOG: stages 0.7.21.2 to 0.7.21.6 rewritten to the model. AGENTS.md lists ADR-0025.

### 0.7.20.1 - The element menu's frame, and the calendar's size and defaults - 3 October 2026

- The element menu held every setting in one narrow column under two tabs. It now has three areas, Content, Style and Placement, as columns in a wide menu and as tabs in a narrow one, with a button in its head that switches and the admin setting «Element menu» for the width it opens in (ADR-0016 addendum). An area without settings takes no column.
- Placement holds what stood at the foot of Style: the narrow-screen fit, «Hide on mobile» and the pinning stand open, motion and the frame are groups that show their value while closed.
- A quick row above the areas carries the settings used most: the calendar's design, max count and subscribe button, and for every block the narrow-screen fit and whether it shows on a phone.
- The calendar's design picker unfolded inside one column. The «Design» row opens it over the whole menu, six thumbnails across, with a button back.
- The floating menu lay over the admin's own panels. It now stays to the right of them, and is narrow whatever is chosen where the wide one does not fit there.
- The narrow-screen choice could not be switched back: the selected block's snapshot left out `fit` and `fitMin`, so the menu always read «wrap». Both are in the snapshot.
- The calendar's colour slots stood one per row. They stand in rows of swatches with the name under each.
- A new calendar wrote the category filter and the subscribe button as on from three places (the block's defaults, the block palette's entries and the section presets). All three write them off, with the sign-up buttons; the sign-up buttons show only with `showSignup: true`; the example page's calendar is switched off too.
- Glass and Dark glass drew a solid ground. Glass is transparent by default and Dark glass a blurred, half transparent dark, so the section shows through; the «Ground» slot sets a solid one.
- The outline did not follow a change of design. The editor asks the preview for the height the content needs after a change of design or settings (`urd-fit-block`, answered with `urd-grow`) and redraws the section at it; the measure is the design's drawn box, so Glass's colour blobs no longer count. After a drag the frame is kept and only grows when the content needs more.
- A «Coming up» design starts with three events in the card and three under «Later» when it is chosen and the counts are unset.
- One «Text» slot coloured the words on both the card and the panel of Coming up on cream. The panel has `panelText`; the same walk through every design is 0.7.19.28.
- The calendar has a «Size» in the Style tab (`scale`, 0.4 to 2, pure `calScale`): the whole design, text included, drawn smaller or larger. A drag of the outline changes the box only.
- The calendar's announcement switch reads «Show the calendar's announcement», and its tooltip says it is separate from the strip above the menu.
- BACKLOG: milestone 0.7.20 with its six stages, milestone 0.7.21 (the sizing model for every element, from the look at how other builders resize), stage 0.7.19.28 and the agreed order of work under «Liten huskeliste».

### 0.7.19.9 - Settings per calendar design - 3 October 2026

- The designs had no settings of their own. `options` on the block holds them (`CAL_OPTIONS`, pure `calOptions` and `calOptionDefs` in calendar-designs.js), and the Style tab shows the section «Settings for the design» under the design picker, only with the options of the chosen design.
- Month with side panel: the panel to the right, to the left or under the month. Week plan and Day plan: a first and a last hour in place of 8 and 18, and a tinted weekend. Table: the Time and Place columns and the striped rows as switches. Poster wall and Picture cards: two, three or four columns. Year wheel: the month at the top. Numbered programme: 1 or 01. Programme booklet and Split card: the description on or off. Band: rolling on or off.
- Coming up bento drew one featured event whatever `nextCount` said; its day tiles now follow `nextCount` and `laterCount` together.
- The «Done when» lines of milestone 0.7.19 in BACKLOG stood collected at the foot of the milestone; each now stands under the stage it belongs to.

### 0.7.19.8 - The parts of the calendar artboards left out - 3 October 2026
