### 0.7.19.1 - The calendar card, named sources, and the foundation for the calendar designs - 3 October 2026

Stage 0.7.13.13 and the foundation stage of the new milestone 0.7.19 in one commit (the two share the block, the panel and the bundle).

- A calendar block with several sources showed the same event twice when it stood in two of them, and the «Next» card held one event. The sources are merged with the same start and title shown once (pure `dedupeOccurrences` in ics.js), the card takes `nextCount` events (1-3) with `laterCount` more (0-10) as one-line rows under «Later», and the heading reads «Coming up» for more than one.
- The category filter could only read categories out of the titles («Meeting: Annual meeting»). A source can now carry a name and a colour (`{ url, name, color }`, pure `sourceEntry`): the name is the category of everything in that calendar, and the colour tints its chips and date badges through `--urd-cal-color`. The panel edits one row per source, and «Calendars on the site» lists the sources of the other calendar blocks for adding with a click.
- A new `agenda` view: compact rows under their month (pure `groupByMonth`), counted by the limit like the list.
- An empty calendar showed nothing on a published page. Every view draws a visible empty state, with the owner's own line (`emptyText`) and an icon from the library or none (`emptyIcon`); a visitor on a block without sources sees it too.
- The designs planned on the Design canvas «Urd calendar designs» (36 designs and five button-free variants, 3 October 2026) need one foundation, so each becomes a renderer and a CSS block. The pure `calendar-designs.js` holds the design list with colour slots, static texts and the edge stripe per design, and the block draws every event field with an `urd-cal-f-<field>` class and the owner's style for that field (`fieldStyle`: font, size, weight, italic, underline, colour), the labels and the buttons' words as `.urd-cal-tx` texts rewritten in place in the preview with the text toolbar and stored under `texts`, the colour slots as `--urd-cal-s-<slot>` with the theme as the fallback, the stripe as a switch with a colour (`stripe`) and the «Sign up» buttons as a switch (`showSignup`). A block without the new props renders as before.
- Editor: the calendar's Style tab (design when more than one exists, the slots as colour pickers that follow the theme when empty, the stripe and its colour, a field picker with the field's font, size, weight, italic, underline and colour), the signup switch and «Reset the texts» in Content, and the view hidden when a design fixes it. Keys in nb, en-GB and tr; the site keys «Coming up» and «Later» in nb, en-GB, tr and nn. SCHEMA.md documents the sources' object form and the ten new props; `tests/calendar-designs.test.mjs` holds the design model and its keys against the dictionaries.
- Found during the headless check and fixed before the commit: the slot variables in base.css referred to themselves after a search-and-replace over the calendar section (`--urd-cal-accent: var(--urd-cal-s-accent, var(--urd-cal-accent))`), so every bordered box went black; the theme fallbacks were put back. The card's own colour rule stood after the stripe rule and overrode the stripe's colour; it was moved before it.

### 0.7.13.12 - Animated images kept as they are, and a file source on the video block - 1 October 2026

- Every image upload was drawn through a canvas to webp, which holds one frame, so an animated GIF, an animated WebP or an APNG came out as a still. The upload now recognises an animation from the file's own bytes (pure `animatedImageKind` and `isAnimatedImage` in imageTools.js: the GIF's blocks walked for a second frame, the WebP's extended header read for the animation flag, the PNG for an `acTL` chunk before the image data) and keeps the file as it is, published under its own extension. It is warned about above 1 MB and refused above 4 MB with a message naming the size and the limit, in the panels and in the preview's image editor. The check sits in `compressToWebp`, so every upload path has it.
- The video block could only embed YouTube and Vimeo; a file existed only as a background layer. `source: 'file'` draws a self-hosted mp4 or webm from media/ as a native `<video>` with the browser's controls, `poster`, `loop` and `muted`; `autoplay` takes effect only with `muted` and never under reduced motion or while editing (pure `videoPlayback`). The film and the poster are written to media/ at publish, inside the video layer's limits (the two upload paths now share `readVideoFile`). Additive props, the block stays at version 1.
- Editor: the video block's Source as a segment, with the file's rows shown only for a file and the self-start only while muted (unmuting clears it). Keys in nb, en-GB and tr; SCHEMA.md documents the block's props and the animated images.
- Found while reading `_headers` for the stage, not fixed: the policy has no `media-src`, so video and audio fall back to `default-src 'self'` and an unpublished film or sound file (a data URL in the draft) is blocked in the preview of a deployed site until it is published. It applies to the video layer and the audio block as well. Recorded in BACKLOG under Bugs.

### 0.7.13.10 - The pattern layer and section shape dividers - 1 October 2026

- A section had no texture but grain and no edge but a straight line. New background layer `pattern` (`backgrounds/pattern.js`): eight drawn tiles (dots, grid, diagonal stripes, checks, waves, zigzag, crosses, triangles) with `color`, `size`, `opacity`, `rotation` and `invert`. The layer paints its box with the colour and uses the pattern as a CSS mask, one SVG as large as the layer with the tile as an SVG `<pattern>`, so a theme token follows the theme, which a colour written into an SVG image could not; without mask support it draws nothing.
- New section field `divider { top?, bottom? }`: a drawn edge over the background and under the content, each with `shape` (wave, tilt, curve, triangle, zigzag), `height` (16-240 px, three fifths on a phone), `color`, `flip` and `invert`. The pure parts live in `divider-model.js`, which the renderer draws from and the editor's panel reads its lists from.
- The comparison maps were checked against the stage: the survey names Elementor's dividers with invert, which the first build lacked; `invert` fills what the outline leaves open, so a peak becomes a notch. The maps themselves were stale after the 0.7.13 deliveries: C19, C20, C21 and C23 in FUNKSJONSKART carry their delivered marks, and the «Urd i dag» counts were taken again (23 core blocks, six gallery views, 35 section presets, eight background layers); ELEMENTKART and LAERDOMMER say what is delivered.
- Editor: Pattern in the layer picker for sections, the menu and the footer, with its own rows; «Edge shapes» under the section's background, where an edge's settings show only while it has a shape. Keys in nb, en-GB and tr.
- Schema in three places: `divider` and `dividerEdge` in page.schema.json, SCHEMA.md (the layer and the field), and the example data (a wave at the foot of the first Kaker section, a dot pattern on the Kontakt section). Two new modulepreload links in the shells.

### 0.7.13.9 - Gallery views mosaic and polaroid - 1 October 2026

- The gallery block had no wall of mixed tiles and no framed cards, ApeironLF's hero styles B and C. Two new views of the same block: `mosaic`, the grid's wall with rows of one height (`rowHeight`) and tiles one cell, two wide, two high or both, dealt per picture from `seed`; and `polaroid`, every picture in a card with a wider foot, leaning by its own throw of the seed inside `tilt` degrees, with `frameColor` for the card and `captions` writing the image text in the foot. Both share the grid's columns and gap, grow with their content, and keep the tiles' lightbox, links and image editor.
- The spans and the leans are the background layer's die: `mosaicSpan` and `frameTilt` are exported from gallery-layout.js (the layer's `mosaicSpans` and `photoLayout` now call them, their throws unchanged), and gallery-model.js builds `tileSpans`, `mosaicWall` and `polaroidTilts` on them.
- A mosaic left to the browser's own packing ended with a hole where the pictures ran out. The pure `mosaicWall` places every tile itself, first-fit row by row, and gives the empty cells to a neighbouring tile; a hole no neighbour can take makes the last large tile a single cell and packs again. Tested over the columns, counts and seeds: no overlap and no hole.
- Found in the testing: the block's host class `urd-gallery-mosaic` was already the background layer's, whose rules set the row height and the gap over the block's own. The block's two views carry `urd-gallery-wall` and `urd-gallery-cards`.
- The Style tab: the view list comes from `GALLERY_VIEWS`, the columns and gap show for the three grid views, the mosaic gets its row height and Shuffle, the polaroids their lean, Shuffle (hidden when the lean is 0), frame colour and caption switch. Keys in nb, en-GB and tr; SCHEMA.md documents the views.

### 0.7.0.36 - The dev-minor bump with its rebuilt bundle - 1 October 2026

- Dependabot PR #23 (the dev-minor group) failed the build-conformity check, since a bot cannot rebuild the bundle and the svelte runtime is part of it. Taken locally instead of merged, so the lockfile and the rebuilt bundle land in one commit: svelte 5.57.0 to 5.57.1, vite 8.3.0 to 8.3.1, @sveltejs/vite-plugin-svelte 7.3.0 to 7.3.1. The PR closes itself when the versions reach main. The editor starts and edits locally with the new runtime (checked 1 October 2026).
- `npm audit` reported one moderate advisory on fast-uri (a transitive dev dependency of ajv, used only by the schema validation): the lockfile lifted to fast-uri 3.1.8, audit clean.

### 0.7.0.16-35p - Push preparation: the picture routes hardened, and the fix round's own regressions - 1 October 2026

- Independent review of the span by three fresh agents, each with its own field (the commits 0.7.0.16 to 0.7.0.34, the security and contracts of 0.7.13.8, and a regression hunt in the fixes of 0.7.0.35). Deviation from the ritual: the first single review agent stopped on a session limit before reporting, and the review was started again in three scoped parts.
- The picture route served SVG from the site's own origin: `/api/photo` passed any `image/*` on with only `nosniff`, and a Function's answer carries no site CSP, so an SVG placed in a share on an allowlisted host would run script with the publisher's cookie (found by running the handler against a mocked upstream). The route passes raster types only (`RASTER_TYPE`, the type written from the allowlist, never echoed), and the answer carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`; tested.
- Redirects were followed before any check (`redirect: 'follow'`), the final host judged afterwards for pictures and never for a Nextcloud or JSON listing. `fetchWithTimeout` follows by hand: each hop must be plain https on the same host or an allowed one before the request goes out, the share's credentials stay with their host, and the abort covers the body too; tested with a 302 off the allowlist, to http, to an address literal and to a port. `SHARE_FILE` refuses the dot segments, and the WebDAV listing is split on its opening tag rather than matched lazily.
- The routes' «shared cache» did not exist: a Function's answer is not cached on its headers alone. `edgeCached` asks and fills the platform's edge cache, after the own-site check and for good answers only; tested with a stand-in cache. The width cap was claimed for every source but only Google resizes: the comment, SCHEMA.md and both photo guides say so, with the 12 MB cap and the raster-only rule.
- The editor showed «{host}» and «{status}» literally in the folder readout, since the client kept only `error` and `code`; both ride along now, and the JSON provider names its host. A stored version 1 gallery layer drew as the fill while the panel showed Floating (the editor never lifts): the panel reads such a layer through the layer's own lift (`galleryView`). The 1 -> 2 lift gave a layer that stored neither the new defaults (opacity 0.85, 12 seconds) instead of what it was drawn with; it pins 1 and 6, tested. The fill fetched its first folder picture twice, once without a width. An empty or failed folder drew nothing inside the failure window but the uploads before it; both fall back to the uploads. The order choices name and newest were offered for an album and a list, which have neither; those sources get «as in the album» and random. `navClip` was still written by a preset and listed in SCHEMA.md, which also said five styles and named a `photos` layer.
- Regressions from 0.7.0.35, found by measuring in headless Chromium against the parent commit: `overflow: clip` on the stripe let the gallery's ribbon band grow to its own track (a grid item's automatic minimum), which doubled the copies on every measurement up to the cap, 258 048 px and 1024 images in a 601 px block; `min-width: 0` on the stripe. The cloned gallery copies kept the load guard's `visibility: hidden` without its listener and stayed blank, and being inert they took no click; the gallery builds each copy from its own tile factory, out of the tab order rather than inert. The sheet's tool restore inserted the last tool before a burger that stood first, breaking the order it was meant to keep; a tool without a next sibling is appended. sticky.js still read 0 for the column under a page-wide strip. The merged clear-and-shrink transition out-ranked both reduced-motion resets. The burger and the border read the desktop values while the class came from the mobile override. The menu-row drag's `dropEffect` check cancelled every release outside the list as well as Escape; the admin document accepts the drop while a row is dragged. The launcher panel's press flag did not reset on `pointercancel`, the picker's choosing flag not on reopening, the hidden empty cluster missed the editor's mobile view, and the one-copy sway drifted out past the band's left edge (centred now, swaying both ways).
- From 0.7.0.16 to 0.7.0.34: a panel remembered from the last visit (History, Updates) opened without its data, since only the toggle called the loaders; `init` loads them. The mobile fold offered «content width» (no rule applies it at phone width) and «over the top section» for the floating forms and the column, where neither does anything; the first is gone and the second is shown for the bar only. The pill hover's negative margin reached the submenu's links and pushed the highlight past the card; top-level items only. A resize of a grown block added the old growth to the new height and snapped back on release; the redraw takes the taller of the frame and the content's last measured height (`pushGrownOf`). The block menu could flip upwards off the top of the screen; it scrolls inside the room above the pointer. The English and Turkish tooltips quoted the Norwegian option label; four comments named the panel in Norwegian and three told what would otherwise happen.
- Review notes, no change: a request with neither `Sec-Fetch-Site` nor `Origin` (curl, old browsers) passes the own-site check, the same trade-off as the auth prologue, at the cost of quota only (no secret is reachable); a Nextcloud share and a listed picture are proxied at their stored size, where a preview endpoint could resize.

### 0.7.0.35 - The pre-push check of the 0.7.13 stages - 1 October 2026

- Check of stages 0.7.13.3 to 0.7.13.7 before the push, by three fresh agents reading each stage from model to render, CSS, editor, schema, SCHEMA.md and locales. Nothing was a blocker; the findings below were fixed in this commit.
- Stage 3 and 4: `atTop: clear` with `scroll: shrink` replaced the shrink transition with the surface fade (same specificity, later rule), so padding and font size jumped; the two are one transition list now. The top-zone surface was not recomputed when the dropdown menu opened or closed, so the panel hung from a transparent bar; `setMobileOpen` refreshes it. The sheet's blur switch did nothing, since `navSurface` dropped `blur: true`; carried both ways, with a test. Closing the sheet put the theme and cart buttons back at the end of the cluster, breaking `tools.order`; each button returns to its own place. The sheet's `touchmove` lock was a non-passive listener on document for every mobile visitor and cancelled touch scrolling inside the cart drawer opened over the sheet; it lives only while the sheet is open and lets a dialog over the sheet through. Un-folding the bar after the expanded mobile menu left every desktop submenu open; they close with the panel. The theme-in-sheet option showed while the theme button was taken out of the cluster.
- Stage 5 and 5.2: a click anywhere outside the side column closed every submenu that `sideSubs: expanded` had opened, and without the arrow nothing could reopen them; the outside click leaves fixed-open submenus alone, and the inert opener leaves the tab order (`tabindex -1`, `aria-disabled`). Dragging the last child out of a menu parent without a link of its own left an item with no target, invalid against the schema; the parent takes the front page, as `removeNavChild` does. Pinned blocks measured the whole nav host and sat a strip too low when the announcement scrolls away; sticky.js reads `--urd-nav-h`. With a column and `place: page` the menu height read 0, so the section toolbar and pinned blocks slid under the fixed strip; the strip is the column's clearance now, and the root `--urd-announce-h` is written only when a strip stands over the sections (a strip inside the column no longer costs the gallery layer a band). A cancelled menu-row drag (Escape) still moved the row, and the drag carried the row key as plain text; the drop effect is checked and the data has its own type. `tools.side: start` put the burger last, not at the outer edge; a cluster with nothing but the burger still took its gap and margin (`:empty` never matched); `tools.align: spread` with one tool sat at the start edge.
- Stage 6: the launcher's `title` went into the button through `innerHTML`; it is text now. Every launcher setting was shown and writable while the launcher was off or absent: a button icon threw on the missing object and a design change switched the launcher on; the settings sit behind the switch, like the cart's, and the helpers never create `show`. The MarkPicker's file input lived inside the open menu, and the OS file chooser's window blur closed the menu before `change` could fire, so an upload never landed; the input lives outside the menu and the blur during a choice is ignored. A click on the fallback panel's padding closed it (`focusout` with no related target); a press inside is let through. The tool order arrows assumed three tools; a hidden theme card is stepped over. An empty shortcut target was not marked red although the menu dims it; the schema no longer demands a non-empty label the editor could not keep. The JSDoc, SCHEMA.md and the schema descriptions still said rows without a usable target were dropped.
- Stage 7: the band could not grow with its words (`height: 100%` with `overflow: hidden`), so a large size was clipped and stacked stripes squeezed the words to nothing; `min-height` and `overflow: clip`, and the block reports the height the text needs. The loop had a gap whenever one copy of the content was narrower than the band; the track now holds as many copies as the band needs per period (`ribbonPeriods`, `syncTrackCopies`, tested), in the gallery's ribbon too, where the copies are `inert` so the hidden tiles no longer take keyboard focus. The ticker's jumps divided the width evenly and stopped mid-word; the block measures each word into keyframes of its own. A stripe of marks stood still when the word list was empty; each stripe decides for itself. Sway showed the words twice; it drifts with one copy. `stripePlace: edge` applied with both thin stripes off, and the stripe colour pickers showed `accent-text` while an unset stripe inherits the block's ink.
- Text: `followNav` and `place` in the `nav.announcement` signature and the sheet's real surface fallback in SCHEMA.md; a Norwegian panel comment, four «would otherwise» comments and two comments left above the wrong code; the radius field's placeholder and a variant tile that cleared the radius when clicked again; a rejected page slug no longer clears the typed title; `nav.launcher`, `nav.launcherMore` and `nav.launcherLess` in the Nynorsk site dictionary.

### 0.7.13.8 - Background motion, the image gallery layer and pictures from shared folders - 1 October 2026

- The image layer moves on its own: `motion` (`kenburns`, `drift`) with `motionSpeed`, on version 3. Parallax already owns `transform` on the carrier, so a moving layer paints on an inner element, and a still one renders the DOM it always did.
- The 2 -> 3 migration sets the defaults first and the stored props last: the editor writes into layer props without lifting them, so a motion chosen on a layer still stored as version 2 was undone at every render. Found in the browser, pinned by a test.
- The image gallery layer (`slideshow`, version 2) is one layer with four layouts on ApeironLF's hero-gallery sizes: frames floating over the section (the default), one picture filling it, rolling bands and a mosaic wall. The separate photos layer built first in the round was folded into it, and its polaroid became a look.
- Every picture wears a shape (fifteen, cut with clip-path or drawn with a rounding, on skin and face alike so a frame follows the shape), a look (thirteen: shadow, polaroid card, borders in a chosen colour, glow, tape, pin, stamp, film strip, soft edge) and a tone (twelve filters, duotone in the accent colour); the three combine freely and come down from the host's classes to every layout's frames.
- The motions per layout: drift and rise travel from outside one edge to outside the other in container units, Ken Burns breathes the whole frame, the cross-fade fades each frame on its own cycle and brings it back elsewhere with the picture that has waited longest, and the bounce runs two axes on two periods; half a second to ninety per cycle. A band rolls by itself, and its track had to be freed from the ribbon block's editing hold, which had kept it still in the preview.
- The count runs to twenty, with a choice for a folder that holds fewer: stop at the pictures there are, or repeat them. The scatter is random on every visit, one seed per page load shared with the random order, and can be fixed with Shuffle; its cells are dealt in seeded order so the empty ones move.
- The area the pictures use: switches for behind the menu bar (on) and behind the announcement (off), coupled since the strip sits above the menu. The layer's box is moved down rather than clipped, so the scatter, the travel and the bounce all work inside the smaller area, and a frame's centre is held half a frame from every edge so none hangs over one. Found in the testing: a frame cut at the menu's edge read as a picture behind the menu.
- Example pictures: a layer with nothing in it draws example pictures in every layout, and the plain image layer draws one, shown inside the editor only, so the whole setup can be made before the photos arrive.
- Pictures from a shared folder: a Google Drive folder, a Google Photos shared album, a Nextcloud or ownCloud public share, or any `.json` list, recognised from the pasted address (pure `photo-source.js`). The list comes from `/api/photos` and every picture from `/api/photo`, so `DRIVE_API_KEY` stays in Cloudflare, the CSP is untouched and nothing is hotlinked; Nextcloud and JSON hosts wait for `PHOTO_HOSTS`, and both routes answer the site's own pages only. The proxy answers with a pool of up to 200 sorted by name or newest, and the layer cuts its own count from it, or draws at random with a fresh set per visit.
- The width follows the use: a full-width background asks for the window's width, a small frame for its own, on the ladder the routes serve, snapped up so nothing is scaled up.
- The review of the round found, and the round fixed: an absent width fetched every picture at 480 px; the Google Photos album key was dropped on the way to Google; renders while a call was out started another; a failure was kept for ten minutes; the open providers could reach Google's hosts; the slideshow's props had changed without a version; the Sami dictionary lacked the new api codes.
- The image guard admits the site's own picture route, the one place a query string is allowed, and refuses everything else with one as before.
- Presets `hero-image` (a photo that creeps, with a veil under the text) and `hero-photos` (floating frames behind centred text), drawn in their cards from the layer's own layout.
- The guide «Pictures from a shared folder» in English and Norwegian, linked from the READMEs, with the two Cloudflare variables as items 10 and 11 of the setup guide.

### 0.7.13.7 - Ribbons - 30 September 2026

- The element review's frieze was missing: a line of words rolling across the page. New core block `ribbon` with the words as items and a separator between them, drawn as a mark (dot, dash, slash, star), written as own text, or nothing. The band is up to three stripes, a thin one above, the main one and a thin one below, and each holds the words, the separator over and over, or colour alone, so a rule over a rolling line is one block; each thin stripe takes its own colour, and `stripePlace` either gives them a share of the height or lays them on the band's own edges.
- The motion is the background loop layer's idiom: the track holds the content twice inside a clipping row and is translated by half its width, so the loop has no seam, and the copy is `aria-hidden` so the words are read once. Four motions: roll, sway, a ticker that jumps one word at a time, and none. The duration is measured, the width of one copy divided by the speed, except the ticker, which is timed per word.
- The band can leave the content surface: `width: 'page'` measures the section and offsets the block to reach the window's edges, rather than `100vw`, which would count the scrollbar. The measuring had to move into the frame after insertion, since render runs before the block is in the DOM and the section lookup came back empty.
- The words are typed straight in the band as well as in the panel's list, and the panel's Style tab is sorted into five cards: the motion, the stripes, form and width, the text and the colours.
- The same motion as a gallery view: `view: 'ribbon'` rolls the images in a band, one row or two running against each other, with its own band height, speed, direction, pause and soft edges. The tiles are the gallery's own, so the lightbox and the image style vocabulary work as in the other views.
- Five lists are maintained by hand before a core block can be inserted (the registry, the canvas menu, the seed, the palette search and the name in Properties), and nothing failed when one was forgotten: the block simply never appeared. `tests/palette.test.mjs` reads the four lists as text and holds them against the registry, with the two deliberate exceptions named.
- Found in the testing: the outlined words were invisible, since the stroke was `currentColor` while the fill was made transparent, so the stroke went transparent too; the block now passes the resolved text colour as `--urd-ribbon-ink` and the stroke takes that. The ticker looked dead because its duration came from the width, which held the first word for several seconds; it is timed per word now, with the dwell as its own setting.
- Found in the testing: the band stood still in Clean view. The hold while editing was a class decided at render, and Clean view is a class toggle on the body with no re-render, so the class stuck. The hold is a body rule now, and the panel's «Play the motion» sets `urd-motion-demo` for six seconds; the gallery's band had the same fault and the same fix.

### 0.7.13.6 - The link launcher - 30 September 2026

- The menu had no way to hold the addresses that should be one click away without standing in it: the owner's other sites, a booking page, the shop's Instagram. A launcher button in the tool cluster (`nav.launcher`) opens them as a panel of tiles, drawn as nine dots until an icon or an own image is set. The panel is a popover in the top layer where the browser can anchor it under the button and flip it away from the edge, and the submenu's disclosure box otherwise (ADR-0011); it scrolls, so the number of shortcuts has no limit.
- The same shortcuts in the mobile menu are a section at the foot of the list that travels into the full-screen sheet with it. The list there is the page's own, so only the first `mobileMax` (1-24, default 6) stand open and the rest sit in a native `<details>` fold.
- Three designs for the panel, chosen for the desktop (`view`) and the mobile menu (`mobileView`) separately: tiles in three columns, one row per shortcut, or image cards in two columns. One markup, and the class on the list carries the design. The heading above them is optional (`showTitle`) and takes the owner's own words (`title`).
- Every shortcut and the button itself take a drawn icon or an own uploaded image, materialized into media/ at publish like the logo. The icon dropdown and the separate upload button are replaced by one mark picker with two tabs, Icons with a search over the library and Images with an upload tile plus the marks already in use, opening on the tab the mark comes from. A choice leaves the menu open, so several marks can be tried; Escape or a click outside closes it.
- The theme button, the cart and the launcher were set up in three different places, and the theme button had no switch at all. The Nav panel gathers them in one Tools group where each tool carries its own switch beside its own settings, `nav.style.tools.theme` takes the theme button out of the cluster, and `nav.style.tools.order` sets the tools' order, moved with the arrows in each tool's header so the panel's order is the menu's.
- Testing found the launcher gone from both menus although it was switched on: every row had a target the link guard rejects, and rows without a usable target were dropped, taking the button with them. The switch alone decides now: every row is drawn, a rejected target gives the tile `#` and a dimmed state, and the editor marks the field red with what a target must look like.
- The chosen button icon drew at the full size of the button, while the nine dots and an own image behaved. The icons in the library are drawn at 100 percent of their parent (icons.js), and the button was missing the size rule the cart has; the tile in an open editor row stretched the same way and now keeps its own height.
- The sorting in 0.7.0.34 rewrote the incoming suggestions instead of keeping them: each moved line now carries the original Norwegian wording as well, the merged ones as their own sub-items, and the arrow-key line that was deleted rather than ticked is back, struck as delivered in 0.7.0.32.

### 0.7.0.34 - The suggestions sorted into the backlog - 29 September 2026

- The Suggestions section had grown to 23 unsorted lines, so work that belongs to a milestone was invisible from that milestone. Twenty-one moved to the milestone or phase that owns them, each keeping its own wording with a dated note, one was ticked as delivered in 0.7.0.32, and three new stages under 0.7.13 take the nine nav and mobile-menu lines; the section keeps the single line that has no milestone yet.

### 0.7.0.33 - The help card follows the admin - 29 September 2026

- The help card the «?» chip opens behaved as though it belonged to the page being built: it had no counter-scale, so at 79 percent preview zoom its text drew at 9.45 px against the admin rail's 11.84, and its surface was one hardcoded colour whatever admin theme was chosen. It counter-scales now, like the text toolbar and the image editor, with the placement measuring the drawn size instead of the laid-out one, and takes `--urd-admin-surface` and `--urd-admin-text`, which the editor already reports to the preview: measured at 12 px text and 300 px wide on screen at 25, 50, 100 and 150 percent zoom, and the rail's own colour in two themes.

### 0.7.0.32 - Drawn carets, and the block menu takes the keyboard - 29 September 2026

- The folds in the «+ New element» menu drew their arrow as the characters ▾ and ▴, which is content and not chrome. A shared `foldToggle` builds the button with the label and a drawn caret that turns over while the fold is open (the FAQ block's chevron pattern), and the open state is a class, so the label survives the click.
- The same two characters stood in the canvas dropdown and in the admin's own. Both draw the caret now, sized in em so it keeps following the button's text, and the admin one turns over while the menu is open.
- A search for «Shapes» in the block menu found nothing: every row is registered with its visible label, so the shapes are registered as «Dash», «Arrow» and the rest, while the fold's own name was registered nowhere. Each row now carries the name of the fold it belongs to and the search reads both, so a group name finds its rows.
- The block menu had no keyboard choice: the search field took only Enter and Escape. The arrow keys now walk a marked row through the menu, the hits while a search is running and the menu's own rows before that, with Enter taking the walked row and otherwise the first hit as before. The step is geometric rather than by document order, since the menu is a two-column grid with rows that span both columns.

### 0.7.0.31 - The block toolbar's buttons take their clicks again - 29 September 2026

- The buttons in a block's toolbar (delete, duplicate, show on mobile, z-order) did nothing when clicked: the icons have been drawn as inline SVG since 0.7.0.20, and the preview's guards read `event.target` as an HTMLElement, which an SVGElement is not, so a click inside an icon slipped past them and into the block's surface drag, whose pointer capture moved the release off the button. The target is read as an `Element` now, through one `eventTarget` helper used by the surface drag, the selection, the marquee and the link interception.

### 0.7.0.30 - The pill highlight stops widening the menu - 29 September 2026

- Choosing the pill hover style pushed the menu items apart: the pill's side padding was added between them, so the row grew by twice the padding at every gap. A horizontal menu now draws the pill around the item instead, giving the padding back as a negative margin, and caps it at half the item spacing so two pills can at most meet. Measured: the distance from one item to the next is 56 px with the pill and without it at the default spacing, and 76 px with both at a spacing of 40. The column and the two mobile menus stack the items and keep the pill as it was.

### 0.7.0.29 - The mobile menu's own setup, the panel - 29 September 2026

- The Mobile fold got the rest of the setup for the phone alone: size, item placement, the tool cluster's side, the content width, the overlay and the border, each starting on «Same» and only then writing a value of its own. A border set there takes its own width and colour, and «None» removes the desktop border on the phone without touching it elsewhere.
- The tool cluster's alignment was in the schema from the previous commit but can never apply on a phone (it is the column's), and is removed rather than left inert. A contract test now ties the schema's mobile keys to the keys the engine reads, so a setting can no longer be added in one place and do nothing in the other.

### 0.7.0.28 - The mobile menu's own setup, the engine model - 28 September 2026

- `nav.style.mobile` held only the thickness and the text size, and the appearance functions had no breakpoint knowledge at all, so a mobile-only layout could not be expressed. It now also takes `size`, `layout`, `inset`, `overlay`, `tools` and `border` (with `side: "none"` to drop the desktop border on the phone alone). `effectiveNav` in nav-model.js merges the overrides into one resolved nav before `navClasses`, `hostClasses` and `navSizeVars` read it, so the pure functions stay free of breakpoints and the choice is made at render time, which is what ADR-0023 requires: the burger class is also set by desktop content folding.
- The floating-only fields and the column's own fields get no mobile override: the phone renders as a bar with a burger, where they do nothing. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit, with three tests on the resolution. The panel controls follow in the next commit.

### 0.7.0.27 - The announcement inside the full-screen menu - 28 September 2026

- The announcement sat behind the full-screen mobile menu and could not even be dismissed there: the strip lives in the nav host, while the menu is a modal dialog in the top layer. New `nav.style.sheetAnnounce` (default false) moves the strip into the top of the menu while it is open and back to the bar on close, the same move-in and move-out the logo already uses. The menu's clearance is left alone while the strip is away and measured again on close, and a strip dismissed from inside the menu stays gone. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit, with a checkbox in the Mobile fold shown only when there is an announcement.

### 0.7.0.26 - What the scroll shrink reaches, and how fast - 28 September 2026

- «Shrink the menu» only reached the padding and the logo: the menu's text size was written as an inline `font-size`, which no CSS rule can scale. It is a variable now (`--urd-nav-text-size`, with the size presets handing theirs over the same way), and the text and the item spacing follow the shrink at half the padding's rate (`--urd-nav-shrink-soft`): with the default factor the padding halves while the text goes from 13.6 to 10.2 px, where the full factor would have left it at 6.8.
- New `nav.style.shrinkAt` (0-400 px, default 80): how far down the page the shrink starts. It used to share the 80 px top zone with the transparent-at-top surface and the hide, so it could not be moved on its own.
- New `nav.style.shrinkMs` (0-1200 ms, default 220): how long the shrink takes, through `--urd-nav-shrink-ms` to the four transitions that carried the duration as a literal. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit, with two sliders in Behaviour under the shrink choice.

### 0.7.0.25 - The menu hides on a real scroll, and the clear surface is renamed - 28 September 2026

- «Hide, show on scroll up» took the menu away at the first notch: the state flipped on the direction of a single frame, since the caller moved the reference point on every frame and no travelled distance existed anywhere. `navScrollState` now owns a turning point that follows the page up while the menu is visible and down while it is hidden, and returns the next one; the menu leaves after 96 px down and comes back after 16 px up, measured from the furthest point in that direction.
- The nav behaviour «Surface appears on scroll» named the event rather than the state: renamed «Transparent at the top of the page» (nb: from «Flaten kommer når man blar» to «Gjennomsiktig på toppen av siden»), with tr to match. The Swedish pack still carried the label from before the rename of 0.7.0.16 and is corrected. The data field `nav.style.atTop` is unchanged.

### 0.7.0.24 - The preview's floating panels follow and flip - 28 September 2026

- The image editor closed at the first scroll: the close stood in for following, since panel and grid are placed from the image's viewport rect and nothing re-placed them. It follows the scroll the way the text and multi toolbars do, and closes when the image itself has left the screen (an IntersectionObserver beside the existing ResizeObserver).
- The double-click block menu ran off the bottom of the screen: it measured its width and flipped sideways, but never read the viewport height. It flips upwards when it would pass the bottom edge and the pointer is in the lower half, re-fits when the Shapes or a plugin fold changes its height, and a menu taller than the screen scrolls instead of running off it.

### 0.7.0.23 - The block chrome stays upright, and the guides can be seen - 28 September 2026

- Rotating a block rotated its buttons with it: the chrome are children of the rotated element. The rotation is written as `--urd-block-rot` beside the transform, and the toolbar, the resize corner, the rotate handle, the sticky badge and the mobile pin turn themselves back upright against it.
- The help chip sat under the rotate handle, which took the pointer in that corner, so the block's own help card could not be opened there. The chip moved left of the handle, above it in the stack, and got the preview-zoom counter-scale it was the only block chrome to lack.
- The always-on guides were hard to see: they were the only editing chrome without the counter-scale, so at a strong zoom out the line rendered thinner than a pixel, and they were drawn at half opacity. Line and dash follow the zoom now, at 0.9 opacity; the drag guides keep their magenta and got a thicker line and a stronger halo.

### 0.7.0.22 - The section height and the rotated block - 28 September 2026

- A block dragged towards the bottom of a section stretched the section instead of lying over the boundary: the push pass raised the section to the lowest edge of every block as soon as any block in it had content growth. A block counts towards its section's height only while its design bottom is inside that height; content growing within the height still pushes and still raises the section (ADR-0024 addendum).
- A rotated block jumped sideways at the first pointer move and landed correctly on release: the redraw wrote the design height while the push pass owns the grown one, and on a rotated element a height change is mostly a sideways shift. The grown box is kept beside the shift (`data-urd-grow`, `pushGrowOf`) and put back by `drawFrame`.
- A rotated block dropped in another section landed too high: the stored y was measured off the element's box, which for a rotated block starts above the frame. It is carried across from the source content surface instead.

### 0.7.0.21 - The image editor and the admin panels - 27 September 2026

- The image editor's panel shrank with the preview zoom: it is the only floating chrome in the preview without the counter-scale its twin `.urd-multi-toolbar` carries. It keeps the admin's size now (measured: 260 px of layout drawn at 330 px at zoom 1.27), with a max height and scrolling so a tall panel cannot run off a zoomed-out page, and the placement clamp measures the drawn box.
- The crop grid stayed behind when the page got wider: it was re-measured only by a ResizeObserver on the image, which does not fire when the image merely moves. Widening the preview from 1700 to 2300 px slides the image 300 px sideways at an unchanged width; grid and panel are placed by one `place()` that a window resize also runs, and the editor's listeners hang off one AbortController.
- Clean view closed the admin panels: the rail and the panel were unmounted, so all their folds came back closed. They are hidden instead, the way the top bar already is, and the panel's scroll position is carried across the round trip. A click in the preview while in Clean view also selected the block behind the hidden panels; the selection handler has the same `urd-chrome-off` guard as the marquee.
- New setting in the gear pop, «Panels on a fresh load»: the admin reopens the panel you last had in front, or starts with none (`urd-admin-panels` and `urd-admin-panel-open` in localStorage, only the non-default stored).

### 0.7.0.20 - The blocks, the guides and the drawn chrome - 27 September 2026

- A collection block could not be centred: at 90 % it is 81 of the content surface's 90 grid cells, an odd number, so snapping left it 8 px off centre either way, outside the guides' 5 px tolerance. The wide defaults are 88.89 % (80 cells), which puts the centred position exactly on a cell boundary; the gallery and product blocks carried the same width and the same defect.
- The drag guides had no quarter positions: the content surface's 25 % and 75 % join the midline. The always-on guides were drawn in the section, so their lines measured the window rather than the content surface the snapping uses; they sit in the surface now, on the same quarters.
- The rotate handle, the drag grip, the deletes and the section toolbar's arrows and fit-height button drew text characters. All are drawn SVG (`CROSS_SVG`, `GRIP_SVG`, `ROTATE_SVG`, `FIT_HEIGHT_SVG` beside the existing icons).

### 0.7.0.19 - The canvas: the drag between sections - 27 September 2026

- A block could not be dragged out of a section that had a growing block in it: the live pass fed the dragged block's own position into the section height, so the section stretched after the block and the section below was never uncovered (measured on shop-fp: the source grew from 400 to 525 px during the drag). A block under the pointer is drawn where the pointer holds it but is left out of the section height (`skipBox` in `writePush`).
- A block dropped in another section answered with a full page send, which rebuilds the whole document from `renderPage`; only the two sections are sent now, the same way the collections branch in urd.js already does it.
- The dropped frame's y was measured against the section's outer box and carried the block's push shift, so a drop into the first section under a menu out of the flow landed a menu height too low. Measured against the target's content surface with the shift taken back out.

### 0.7.0.18 - The bar follows the content width by default - 27 September 2026

- «Align to content width» (`nav.style.inset`) was off unless set: the read is now `!== false`, so a new site gets the bar's contents lined up with the content edge. Site schemaVersion 3 to 4 writes `inset: false` into sites that omitted the field, so a site built before the change keeps the menu it was built with (the ADR-0018 precedent: the old default is written in rather than derived at read time). Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit, with migration and navClasses tests.

### 0.7.0.17 - The announcement stays while the bar hides - 27 September 2026

- «Hide, show on scroll up» took the announcement with it: the strip is a child of the host that `urd-nav-hidden` slides out. New `nav.announcement.followNav` (default false) keeps the strip in place: the slide moves from the host to the menu, which travels its own height plus the strip's, and the band the host still occupies takes no pointer events so the page under it stays reachable (`urd-nav-announce-keep`). Shown as a checkbox in the Announcement fold only with hide on scroll on a sticky bar. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit.

### 0.7.0.16 - The nav's scroll behaviour in the preview, and the dismissed announcement - 27 September 2026

- The nav's scroll behaviour was only visible in Clean view: `applyScroll` clamped shrink and hide off whenever the editing chrome was on. It runs in every view now, and the editor chrome (the section toolbar, the boundary chip, the block toolbar's flip) parks against the new `--urd-nav-h-base`, the menu's unshrunk height, so the handles stand still while the menu shrinks.
- A dismissed announcement came back in the preview: the read, the write and the recheck were all switched off there. The preview uses the same key as the published page, and the Announcement fold has «Show the strip again», which clears it (`clearAnnounceDismissal` in nav.js, the message `urd-announce-reset`).
- The nav behaviour «Transparent at the top» named the surface rather than what happens: renamed «Surface appears on scroll» in en-GB (nb: from «Gjennomsiktig øverst» to «Flaten kommer når man blar»), with tr and nn to match. The data field `nav.style.atTop` is unchanged.

## [0.7.4] - 2026-09-26

### 0.7.0.15p - Release 0.7.4: the reference plugins into the core, the content push, the navigation round and the bug round - 26 September 2026

- RELEASE: the engine bumped 0.7.3 to 0.7.4 after the ritual (urd.json, git mv of the engine folder, the assets/urd/ shells and dictionaries, the HTML shells with byte-identical slug copies, editor/package.json and the lockfile, the bundle rebuilt), check-release green in full mode for v0.7.4. Tag v0.7.4 and the GitHub release are done manually after the push, tag last.
- Independent review of the release (a fresh agent in its own worktree with its own build, tests, validation, release check, copies, stamp and em dash checks over the whole diff). No defect and no nit: the 92 engine files are pure renames, the bundle differs only in its 21 engine path strings, and nothing points at the removed folder (`_headers`, the guard's path lists, urd.json's `ownedPaths`, the speculation rules, dev-server.py and the workflows are all version-neutral, and the remaining `0.7.3` mentions in the schemas, SCHEMA.md and the plugin README are history).
- Review note, no change: the entries 0.7.17.1-0.7.18.7p (24 September) and 0.7.0.11-0.7.13.5.2p (26 September) stand in that order from before this release; both belong under the new heading.

### 0.7.17.1-0.7.18.7p - Push preparation: the push model, the width fixes, and the four reference plugins into the core - 24 September 2026

- Independent review of the span (a fresh agent in its own worktree, the whole diff against AGENTS.md and the ADRs with its own build, tests, validation, copies, stamp, i18n, three-places and em dash checks). One defect: a block resized by its handle painted its original height throughout the drag, since the section's ResizeObserver ran the push pass on every height change and the suspended pass wrote the design height back from the frame the drag only updates on release (found by code reading). Fixed: a suspended pass leaves the box alone and only restores the position. A TESTRUNDER item covers the handle.
- Review findings fixed: `nav.style.atTop` was in the schema and SCHEMA.md but not in the example data (site.json now uses `clear`); AGENTS.md's bundled-module list lacked `blocks/form`, which sections/presets.js pulls into the bundle for its seed fields (listed, and the editor's palette now takes the seed from the same function instead of a copy); the Swedish pack was called complete while the keys of 0.7.18.2 to .5 were missing, and 34 older visitor keys (share, shop, countdown) had never been in it (101 admin and 67 site keys added, the pack is complete again); detached section hosts stayed in the push registry after a page switch (pruned when a new section is wired); a form field without `required` showed as required in the panel (now `=== true`); a field id was put into a selector without `CSS.escape`; the class `urd-cal-idag` renamed `urd-cal-today` (ADR-0021); comments that narrated («would otherwise», «the frame follows») and stale plugin mentions in SCHEMA.md, ROADMAP-en-GB and BACKLOG («Nettsted-analyse» is «Besøksmåling») corrected.
- Review risk left as a test item: the push pass changes the size of elements it observes inside the observer callback, which the specification reports as a ResizeObserver loop message when it happens at the wrong depth; the headless probe of 23 September saw none during a stepwise narrowing, and the TESTRUNDER item stands.
- Review note, no change: nine TESTRUNDER lines (the 0.7.0.5 batch and seven 0.7.14 items) left in the commit 0.7.18.2 as struck by the test round, outside the agent's edits in that commit.

### 0.7.0.11-0.7.13.5.2p - Push preparation: the bug round and the column's tools and submenus - 26 September 2026

- Independent review of the span (a fresh agent in its own worktree, the whole diff against AGENTS.md and the ADRs with its own build, tests, validation, copies, stamp, three-places, i18n and em dash checks; the render-blocking engine script judged sound for CSP, element lookup and the fallback without the attribute). No defect. Nits fixed: with the column's submenus open from the start and the arrow on, hover reopened a folded submenu while the open-on choice was hidden (hover open is off in that mode); the page-wide announcement strip fell to z 5002 under the preview's section chrome (it keeps the strip's stacking); a failed boot left a held page transition standing for its five-second ceiling (boot.js ends the hold and logs the failure); the column's Oppe/Nede choice carried the bar's tooltip (its own key); the shape length field accepted 0 (floored at 1); an unused import in nav.js; three comments that narrated the old symptom; a wrong count in the BACKLOG plan; the pin-on-scroll bullet split into its three fixes and a rationale clause dropped.
- Review notes, no change: the parked block's raised stacking lives inside its own section's context; the arrival hold needs `blocking="render"` to be honoured before `pagereveal`, elsewhere the plain cross-fade remains; the rotate handle's glyph in preview-edit.js predates the span (a character in editor chrome, listed for a later round).

### 0.7.13.5.2 - The column's tools and submenus, and the strip above the bar - 26 September 2026

- The bar still painted over the announcement strip in the preview: the preview lifts the bar to z 100001, above the strip's 5001. The strip sits above the bar in every context.
- Tools «Left» with a side column sent the whole menu to the bottom: the cluster moved first in the column while keeping the bottom-parking margin, which pushed everything after it down. In the column `tools.side` now means top (`start`) or bottom (`end`), and the new `tools.align` (`start`, `center`, `end`, `spread`) sets the cluster's alignment across the column; the Layout fold shows Top/Bottom and Alignment for the side variants (Left/Right for the bar as before).
- `nav.style.sideSubs` (`collapsed` | `expanded`): the column's submenus open from the start, like the mobile mode; `nav.style.sideSubArrow` (default false) keeps the open/close arrow, without it the arrows are gone and nothing folds the submenus (no hover closing, no click). Shown in the Submenu fold for the side variants; the open-on choice hides while every submenu is open. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit.

### 0.7.0.14 - The announcement's place with a side column - 26 September 2026

- `nav.announcement.place` (`nav`, `page`, `content`, default `nav`): with a side column the strip sits at the top of the column, fixed across the whole page above column and content (the column and the body start below it by `--urd-announce-page-h`), or in the flow above the content beside the column. Shown as a segment in the Announcement fold for the side variants only; the bar and floating variants keep the strip in the menu. Schema, SCHEMA.md and the example data in the same commit.

### 0.7.0.13 - The blocks: grain to 100 % and a line that can be lengthened - 26 September 2026

- The grain layer's strength stopped at 30 %: only the editor's slider capped it, the engine renders the value as plain opacity. The slider runs 0 to 100 %, and SCHEMA.md documents the layer.
- A line could not be lengthened by its corner: on a 4 to 8 px tall block the rotate handle, drawn inside the frame's top-right corner and counter-scaled by the preview zoom, covered the resize corner at any zoom under about 73 %, so the grip rotated instead. The rotate handle sits above the block on the toolbar's row (below it when the toolbar is flipped under), and the line and arrow get a length field beside the thickness in the Properties panel (`lbl.length` in nb, en-GB, tr, nn).

### 0.7.0.12 - The canvas: the section height after a move, the boundary line, the text toolbar and the pinned block's release - 25 September 2026

- A section's dragged height reset when a block was moved: the resize paths wrote only the inline min-height, and the push pass restored the copy in `data-urd-min-height` on its next measurement. The three resize paths write both (`setMinHeight` in preview-edit.js).
- The dashed boundary line between sections blinked: the «+ New section» bar's 1 px line took pointer events while shown and stole the hover from the section that showed it, restarting the fade at every flip. The line takes no pointer events and the bar shows as a cut.
- The text toolbar covered the block's own toolbar: both sat in the band above the block. The text toolbar now stacks above the block toolbar (and measures its drawn size, so the gap holds at every preview zoom).
- Pin on scroll with a release at a later section: the block parked beyond its own section returned to its frame stacking and was painted behind the next section's blocks. It keeps the pinned stacking while parked there.
- A section with «Lift on hover» took a pinned block with it when hovered (the transformed section became the pin's containing block). A section holding a pinned block no longer lifts.
- The release list offered every section under wrong numbers when the block's section was not found (a slice from -1). It offers the block's own section only.
- The release itself comes at the chosen section's bottom edge, so a release at the last section keeps the block pinned to the end of the page (verified, no change).

### 0.7.0.11 - The nav and the announcement: the hero setting while editing, the dismissed strip, the side column and the page arrival - 25 September 2026

- «Menu as part of the hero until scrolled» (`nav.style.atTop: clear`) looked broken: the scroll pass forced the scrolled surface on while editing, and the Clean-view switch never recomputed it, so the clear surface appeared only after scrolling down and up in Clean view. The top-zone state now follows the scroll position in both views (only the shrink and hide stay Clean-view only), and the switch recomputes it at once (`refreshNavScroll` in nav.js).
- A dismissed announcement came back on the next page: the prerendered document (speculation rules) had built its strip before the cross was clicked. The key is read again when a prerendered or cached document comes on screen, and the strip leaves with it.
- `urd-preview` was added to the body after the first `renderNav`, so the first preview render read the visitor's dismissal key and skipped the side column's resize grip; the class is set before the menu renders, as is `urd-mobile`.
- The example site.json ships with the announcement off: a fresh site starts without a strip.
- The side column showed neither theme button nor cart with an announcement on: the strip pushed the full-height menu below the fold. The column is a flex column and the menu takes the remaining height.
- The bar's top pixels (a logo growing out of the bar) painted over the announcement strip: the strip sits above the bar.
- The page still moved on every page switch: the arriving document is captured by the cross-document view transition before the engine has fetched its data, so the old page cross-faded into the empty shell and the menu popped in afterwards. The engine module is loaded render-blocking from the head, and a held arrival keeps the old page on screen until the new one is rendered, then ends the transition as a cut (`html.urd-arriving` in base.css, the arrival hold in urd.js); the menu and footer names from 0.7.13.5 are gone with the cut rules. The menu's height is measured synchronously at render as well.

### 0.7.13.4-0.7.0.10p - Push preparation: the full-screen menu, the announcement bar, the Nav panel and the discard fix - 25 September 2026

- Independent review of the span (a fresh agent in its own worktree, the whole diff against AGENTS.md and the ADRs with its own build, tests, validation, copies, stamp, three-places, i18n and em dash checks). Two defects, both found by code reading: a menu item with a submenu dropped among another item's children became a nested submenu (invalid against the schema, rendered one level only), and dropped onto its own child rows became its own child, a cycle that made every draft save throw. Fixed: such a drop lands after the parent instead, and a row's own children take nothing; the drop handler carries the same guard. The scrolling announcement pulled the side column up: the fixed side host got the negative sticky top meant for a bar in the flow. Fixed: the side host keeps the strip (nav.js, base.css) and the checkbox is hidden for the side variants.
- Review nits fixed: the whole menu row was draggable and took the mouse from the name field's text selection (the grip alone is draggable now); a view-transition comment in base.css narrated the old symptom; the «no basket» sentence in SCHEMA.md had drifted from `nav.cart` to `nav.style.tools`; a stale two-column comment above the menu list styles.
- Review notes, no change: seven optional announcement and sheet subfields are absent from the example data (the objects are exercised); a grown block is drawn at its design height during a drag and regrows on release, as before the span; the sheet leaves the fold state alone while open.

### 0.7.0.10 - The discard confirmation stays under the pointer - 25 September 2026

- «Sure?» sometimes answered with a jump and discarded nothing: the global `button:active` rule sets `transform`, which replaced the pill's own `translateX(-50%)` while the mouse button was held, so the pill moved half its width to the right on press and a press on its left half was released over the preview iframe (no click, the window lost focus, the pill was disarmed). Found from a console log of the owner's clicks, where the failing presses never produced a click on the pill, and reproduced headless at 15 and 35 % of the pill's width. The pill is now centred with the `translate` property, which the active rule leaves alone.
- A window blur while the pointer is held down on the discard button or the pill no longer disarms, and a completed discard shows «The drafts are discarded» in the status field (`ui.discarded` in nb, en-GB, tr, nn).

### 0.7.13.5 - Announcement bar and tool placement, the Nav panel redesign, and the push drawn live - 25 September 2026

- `nav.announcement` (`show`, `text`, `page` or `href`, `sticky`, `dismiss`, `color`, `textColor`): a strip above the menu inside the nav host (`announcementModel` in nav-model.js, rendered by nav.js); with `sticky` false the host's sticky top is raised by the strip's height so it scrolls away while the menu sticks (`--urd-announce-h`, hosts out of the flow keep it), `--urd-nav-h` counts the menu alone then, and the cross (on by default) hides the strip for the visitor until the text changes. The strip overlaps the bar by one pixel against a hairline seam at fractional preview zoom.
- `nav.style.tools.side`: the tool cluster before the logo (`urd-nav-tools-start`), so the burger sits on the left on mobile.
- Nav panel: the enum choices as segments through the new `Choice.svelte` (two or three short options beside the label, more or longer ones stacked); Logo with the type as a segment, a thumbnail beside the picker and the three sizes on one row; Layout with the six menu forms as drawn tiles; Size with the free values behind an Adjust fold; Frame with the border sides as drawn tiles; Behaviour as two cards (when scrolling, the cart); Mobile stacked with capital labels, the sheet's surface as swatch plus slider, the blur as a switch; Colours with the hover styles as samples of a menu word and the colours as a swatch row (the blur switch moved here); Submenu with the designs as drawings; text fields styled like the other fields, the announcement's text and address filled with the label above; dropdowns beside a label bounded to about 11 rem; the two size fields aligned at the bottom.
- Menu items: one compact row per item with a drawn grip, the name edited in place with its target beneath (compact dropdown), a submenu marker, the actions as a two-by-two grid on the row under the pointer or the selected one; drag and drop with a faint clone at the landing place, into a top-level row (right of its first quarter) as its last child and out again through the grip column of a child row, a release outside the list landing where the clone stood, and no clone where nothing would change. «Create page and add» creates a blank page and adds it as a menu item.
- The full-screen menu: theme button and cart as separate options, icons only unless `sheetToolLabels`, «the sheet» replaced by «the menu» in every label, hover never opens a submenu in either mobile menu, touch scrolling locked to the sheet, and with collapsed submenus a tap on an item opens its submenu instead of navigating (the preview's link listener now skips a cancelled click as well). A variant switch clears `nav.style.radius`, which had made pill, square and tab identical.
- The push (ADR-0024): editing no longer resets the blocks to their design positions; the pass stands still, the drag draws its block with the block's own shift (`pushShiftOf`) and the push is drawn live from the last measurements (`pushPreview`), so nothing jumps at drag start or at release. The calendar's preview-only note is block chrome outside the measured content (it had made the block 62 px taller in the preview than on the published page, and the neighbours were pushed 85 px against 23), and demo data counts as no growth; every section and block now measures the same in the preview and on the published page.
- The menu shivered on every page load: the cross-document view transition morphed the named menu between two pages whose boxes differed by a scrollbar. The menu and footer now switch as a cut, and `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on html gives every page the same width.
- Discard draft: the confirmation is taken in the capture phase on window rather than the pill's delegated handler, and a failed discard is shown in the status field; the sporadic failure itself is still open (BACKLOG, Bugs).

### 0.7.13.4 - Full-screen mobile menu, the Mobile fold, and submenus for touch - 24 September 2026

- `nav.style.mobileMenu: 'sheet'`: the burger opens a full-screen native `<dialog>` that the menu list moves into and back out of (nav.js `buildSheet`, `mobileMenuMode` in nav-model.js); Escape, the focus trap, the focus return and the scroll lock are the browser's, a link click, the cross and a backdrop click close it. An open accordion inside closes alone on Escape (the cancelled keydown keeps the dialog open).
- `nav.style.sheetMotion` (top, bottom, left, right, fade, none; cut under reduced motion), `sheetLogo`, `sheetTheme` and `sheetCart` (the logo into the head, the buttons into a foot with visible labels, all back in place on close) and `nav.style.sheet` as the menu's own surface through `navSurface` (`--urd-nav-sheet-bg`, `--urd-nav-sheet-blur`); omitted = the bar's veil and blur.
- `nav.style.mobileSubs`: `collapsed` (a tap anywhere on an item with a submenu opens it instead of navigating, the item's own page listed first in the submenu, hidden on desktop) or `expanded` (every submenu open from the start, the item a plain link). Hover never opens or closes a submenu in either mobile menu.
- Nav panel: the Mobile fold gathers the mobile thickness and text size (empty = as on desktop), the mobile menu, the sheet's options and surface, and the submenu mode; the Size fold shows the desktop values only. Keys in nb, en-GB, tr and nn; `nav.closeMenu` in the five site dictionaries; schema in three places; the demo uses the sheet.
- The preview's link listener navigated on every link click, so a mobile item that had opened its submenu still switched page in the editor; it now skips a click the link's own handler cancelled (preview-edit.js).

Both got Content panels in the editor: the form's send mode, recipient or endpoint, field list (name, type, required, order, options for select and radio), button text and confirmation; the calendar's sources, view, count (list and cards only) and the two toggles. The gears, their panels and their CSS are gone from `blocks/form.js`, `blocks/calendar.js` and base.css; the help chip sits on the block like elsewhere. ### 0.7.17.5 - The page zoom withdrawn: blocks shrink one by one, with a smallest scale on every block - 23 September 2026

- «Skaler siden» (the section `zoom` of the ADR-0018 addendum) made nothing adapt, everything only got smaller (test finding). Removed: `site.layout.scale` (schema, SCHEMA.md, site.json), scale-model.js with its test and preload link, `--urd-scale` and the section zoom in base.css, the root variable writer in urd.js and every `currentCSSZoom` conversion in sticky.js and preview-edit.js; the Site panel controls and their keys. The addendum stays in ADR-0018 marked withdrawn; ADR-0024 decisions 5 and 6.
- The shrink choice moved from the text block's props to block-level `fit` and `fitMin` on every block (page.schema.json, SCHEMA.md, the Hjem paragraph and the first Om oss image in the example data), one shared row in the Style tab. Content blocks zoom their content to fit the design height; image, video, shape and icon keep a floor on the frame's width instead (`fitFloorPx` and `FIT_BY_WIDTH` in push-model.js, `frameToCss` with a floor capped at the canvas edge; tested). Keys `lbl.fit`, `opt.fit.*`, `tip.fit`, `lbl.fitMin`, `tip.fitMin` in nb, en-GB and tr.
- Three test findings on the text shrink along the way: the floor was applied on top of the section zoom (40 % gave 30 % of the design size), the text shrank with the canvas before it needed to (now a bisection in the push pass that zooms only as much as the design height needs), and each resize step painted the wrapped text once before the fit (the ResizeObserver now runs the pass synchronously, before paint). The floor range opened to 1-100 %.

### 0.7.13.3 - Nav surface: transparent at the top, rounding as a value, the underline seam - 23 September 2026

- `nav.style.atTop: 'clear'`: no surface, blur, border or shadow while the page is in the top zone, fading in after 80 px (`scrolled` in `navScrollState`, `urd-nav-clear` from `hostClasses` for bar and floating, `urd-nav-scrolled` toggled by nav.js; the CSS sets the properties, not the variables, since nav.js writes the surface variables inline). Checkbox «Transparent at the top» (nb «Gjennomsiktig øverst») in Behaviour. The host class was first never written: nav.js toggles host classes from a fixed list, and the new class was missing there (found with headless Brave, fixed).
- `nav.style.radius` 0-64 px for the floating variants (`--urd-nav-radius` from `navSizeVars`, the three variants as presets in the fallback), a number field under Menu width; the bound in `NAV_SIZE_BOUNDS` and nav-size.js with the parity test.
- The underline hover is revealed with `clip-path` instead of `scaleX`, so the end frame equals the resting line.
- Regression from 0.7.17.2 found by the same probe: `setScale` was defined in `boot` but called from the site-draft listener in `enablePreview`, so every Site, Theme and Nav panel change threw before the nav was rendered. Fixed by a module-level `applyScale` (since removed with the page zoom in 0.7.17.5).

### 0.7.17.2 - Scaling below the content width and the content push: zoom to a floor, blocks that move, text that shrinks - 23 September 2026

- Scaling built (ADR-0018 addendum): `scale-model.js` (`pageScale`, `scaleFloorWidth`, tested) and `--urd-scale` on the root; the desktop section carries `zoom: var(--urd-scale)` with `--urd-unzoom` for the nav clearance and the section toolbar; sticky.js and preview-edit.js convert pointer deltas and measurements with `currentCSSZoom`. Site panel: «Below the content width» (scale or fixed) and «Smallest scale» with the floor width; `site.layout.scale` in the schema, SCHEMA.md and site.json.
- The hand-off to the row grid at the floor width was withdrawn after testing (the menu folded there and the nav sizes jumped, and every tablet and small laptop window got the phone layout): below the floor the factor stays there and the canvas is fluid again; the mobile breakpoint is the site's own at every width and the menu folds only by its fold logic or at that breakpoint.
- The real overflow was content, not width: a paragraph that wraps at a narrower window grew its own box over the button beneath, since absolute frames reserve no space. Deep research (LAERDOMMER §2, Squarespace Fluid Engine against the Wix Editor's push rules, the desktop row grid inventoried) and [ADR-0024](adr/0024-content-push-on-the-desktop-canvas.md): `push-model.js` (`pushLayout`, gap 70/10 px, the middle rule, the cascade; tested) and a push pass in render.js that moves the blocks below a grown block on the elements only, grows the section to the pushed bottom, and re-runs on every resize; `suspendPush`/`resumePush` beside the sticky suspension so drags happen on the design; typing growth pushes the neighbours in the data in the same undo step.
- The push did nothing in the preview: the text block and the ten data blocks wrote their measured height into the frame (`urd-grow`), so the push saw no growth. Found with headless Brave over the DevTools protocol at 700 px; the amendment removed, the push pass owns the box height and measures the content with the box at the design height, so a grown box comes back when its content fits again.
- Shrink instead of wrap: a text block can carry `fit: "shrink"` with `fitMin` (1-100 %, default 60 %), «On narrower screens» and «Smallest scale» in the Style tab (nb, en-GB, tr). Three test findings on the way: the floor applied on top of the section zoom (40 % gave 30 % of the design size; the section zoom is now divided out), the text shrank with the canvas before it needed to (now zoomed only as much as the design height needs, a bisection in the push pass, the CSS rule and `--urd-fluid` dropped), and each resize step painted the wrapped text once before the fit (the observer now runs the pass synchronously, before paint).
- Editor: the Screen choice (own window or an editing size) moved from a popover under the Screen button into the admin settings behind the gear; the Reference 1920 device removed; a Screen narrower than the site's mobile breakpoint is mobile to the engine, as the published page is.
- AGENTS.md: the auto-grow lesson rewritten to ADR-0024 (the display grows, the frame never), `scale-model` in the bundled list, ADR-0024 in the list. BACKLOG 0.7.17 staged with a test-round follow-up; TESTRUNDER batch (0.7.17.2-3).

### 0.7.17.1 - Scaling below the content width: the survey and the decision - 23 September 2026

- The narrow-width text folding became its own milestone 0.7.17 (moved out of 0.7.3: it is a desktop problem between the content width and the phone, not a mobile one), with a survey stage, a build stage and a test-round stage.
- LAERDOMMER §2: a subsection on how twelve builders scale below the design width and what the owner controls (Tilda, Readymag, Wix Studio, Squarespace Fluid Engine, Duda, Webflow, Framer, Elementor, Figma Sites, Hostinger, Canva, Carrd, plus CSS `zoom` and WCAG), five rules and «What Urd takes»; the sources in Kilder. FUNKSJONSKART §3 and a C26 row.
- ADR-0018 addendum: the canvas scales proportionally with CSS `zoom` between the binding width and a floor (`site.layout.scale = { mode, min }`, omitted = today's behaviour), and below the hand-off width the page uses the row grid of ADR-0019; `zoom` is allowed on the canvas because it creates no containing block for fixed descendants, `transform` stays forbidden.
